# The Japan Year Book 1914 — Who's Who & Directories

1,753 entries, LLM-transcribed. These entries were transcribed by the vision model of Opus 5.5. Before using any of these details, you must verify specific details with the original source which is linked to whenever possible.


## The Japan Year Book

**Princess Tsuneko** , consort of Prince Hiroyasu, a daughter of Prince Yoshihisa Tokugawa was born Sept. 23rd, 1882; married Jan. 9th, 1896. Issue:—four sons and three daughters. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Prince Kuniyoshi** , 2nd son of Prince Sadanaru, was b. in 1880. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Princess Fumihide** , 7th daughter of Prince Kuni-iye and sister to Prince Sadanaru, was b. in 1844, and in an abbess at the Enjo-ji temple, Nara-ken. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Arisugawa** —The House was founded by Prince Yoshikoto (1603-'38 A.D.) son of the 106th Emperor. The late Prince, 10th of the line, died in July 1913 without leaving a male issue, his only son having preceded him while a naval cadet at Edajima. With the death of the two widow Princesses, the House would become extinct in the ordinary course, but in view of the special relation in which the House stands to the Court, the reigning Emperor has ordered his third son to become a future head of the House. Res. Sannen-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Yasuko** 4th daughter of the late Marquis Maeda, born Feb. 13th, 1864; married to the late Prince, Dec. 11th, 1880. Issue:—a son who died April '08 and a daughter (Princess Isa-ko) who married in '08 to Prince Yoshihisa Tokugawa. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princes Tadako** 7th daughter of the late Count Mizoguchi, born May 12th, 1855; married to the Prince Nori-hito, 9th of the line; widow in 1895. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Kan-in** —The House was founded by Prince Nao-hito (1708-'52 A.D.) eldest son of the 113th Emperor whose 6th son ascended the Throne as Kokaku Tenno, the present head being the 6th of the line. Prince Koto-hito, head, son of Prince Kuni-iye Fushimi and younger brother of Prince Sadanaru Fushimi, was born Sept. 22nd, 1865; studied at the Mil. Boys School '77-81 and at French mil. school, '82-91; Major at the time of the Japan-China War; Lieut.-Col. '95; Maj.-Gen. '01; Lieut.-General (Cav.) '14, and took part in the recent War under Marshal Oyama; appointed Commander of the 1st Army Division Feb. 1906 and grand 2nd class Golden Kite; Full General, and Supreme Councillor of War, Nov. '12; is Hon. President of the Red Cross Society. Res. Nagata-cho, Koj., T. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Princess Chieko** consort, 2nd daughter of the late Prince Sanemi Sanjo, was May 25th, 1872; married to the Prince Dec. 19th, 1891. Issue:—a son and five daughters. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Higashi-Fushimi** —(formerly Komatsu) Prince Yori-hito, and younger brother to Princess Fushimi and Kan-in, 17th son of the late Prince Kuni-iye, was b. Sept. 19th, 1867; studied in England and France 84-90; was second in command on board the cruiser Chitose which with the Tsushima bombarded and sank the Russian cruiser Novic at Korsakof, Saghalien, Aug. 20th, 1904; Captain of the armored cruiser Kasuga, Jan., 1905; attached to the Naval Staff Board '06; 3rd class Golden Kite; Rear Adm. '09; vice-Adm. 13; attended coronation ceremony of King George, V. with his consort. Res. Tame-ike, Ak., Tokyo. — p. 46 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n91/mode/1up))


## Imperial Court

**Princess Kaneko** , consort, 1st daughter of late Prince Tomosada Iwakura; was b. Aug. 26th, 1876, married on Feb. 10th, 1898. Issue :—none. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Kwacho** Prince Hirotada 4th of the line, head, 2nd son of Prince Hiroyasu Fushimi, was b. Jan. 25th, 1902; made to succeed to the House in 1904, in exchange for his father who had represented the House. Res. Mita Dai-machi, T. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Yamashina** Prince Takehiko, 3rd of the line, head, 1st son of the late Prince Kikumaro; was born Feb. 1898. He has four brothers and one sister. Res. Fujimi-cho, Koj., T. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Tsuneko** , mother, 5th daughter of Prince Tadayoshi Shimazu; was b. Feb. 7th, 1874; married to the late Prince Nov. 26th, 1902; widow '08. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Kaya** Prince Tsunenori, 2nd of the line, head, 1st son of the late Prince Kuninori, was b. Jan. 27th, 1902. The Prince has two sisters. Res. Kyoto. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Yoshiko** , eldest daughter of Marquis Daigo; was b. Oct. 20th, 1865; married to the late Prince Nov. 26th, 1892; widow 1910. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Kuni** Prince Kuniyoshi, 2nd of the line, head, 3rd son of the late Prince Asahiko; Col. of Art., was b. July 23rd, 1873; Sub.-Lieut. 1897; was attached to Gen. Kuroki's Staff during the recent war as Major (Inf.) and was granted 4th class Golden Kite; Colonel in '08, studied in Germany '07-10; Maj.-Gen. 13. Res. Kyoto. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Princess Chikako** , consort, 7th daughter of Prince Takayoshi Shimazu; was b. Oct. 19th, 1879; married on Dec. 13th, 1899; travelled through Europe '09-'10. Issue :—three sons and three daughters. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Kuni** Prince Taka, 5th son of the late Prince Asahiko; was b. 1875 in Kyoto; Acting Grand Custodian of the Great Shrine of Ise. Married in 1905 Lady Shizuko, daughter of Viscount Minase. Res. Kyoto. Issue :—a son and a daughter. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Nashimoto** Prince Morimasa, 2nd of the line, head, Maj.-Gen. of Art., 4th son of the late Prince Asahiko; was b. March 9th, 1874; Sub.-Lieut. '97; on the outbreak of the recent War hurried back from France where he was studying at a military academy; was attached to General Oku's Staff as Capt. of Infantry; Major 1906; Lieut.-Col. '08; granted 4th class Golden Kite; Colonel '00. He started for Europe April, 1907 to resume his study, returning home July, '09. Res. Aoyama Kitamachi, T. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Princess Itsuko** , consort, and daughter of Marquis Nabeshima, was b. Feb. 2nd, 1882; married Nov. 28th, 1900, and made tour in Europe '80-'89. Issue :—2 daughters. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Kitashirakawa** Prince Narihisa, 3rd of the line, head, 2nd son of the late Prince Yoshihisa; was b. April 18th, 1887; and married with Princess Kane, 7th daughter of the late Emperor Meiji, Jan. 1890. Is Captain of Artillery. Res. Takanawa Minami-cho, Tokyo. Issue :—a son and two daughters. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Tomiko** , mother, adopted daughter of the late Prince Hisamitsu Shimazu: was b. Aug. 8th, 1862; widow in 1895. Prince Narihisa has two more brothers and four sisters of whom the eldest was married to the son and heir of Count Kanroji in 1904, and the second Princess Takeko to Count Hoshina in 1911. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 92](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n92/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Komatsu** Dowager Princess Yoriko, consort of the late Prince Akihito Komatsu ; 1st daughter of the late Yorishige Arima ; was b. June 18th, 1852 ; and married Nov. 6th, 1869 ; widow Feb. 18th, 1903. Res. Hashiba, Asakusa, T. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 93](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n93/mode/1up))

**Takeda** Prince Tsunehisa, Major, Cavalry, was b. '82; eldest son of the late Prince Kita-shirakawa. The house was newly created March 30th, '06. He married Princess Tsune, 6th daughter of the late Emperor Meiji, Sept. 30th, 1888; was decorated with 5th class Golden Kite. Res. Takanawa Minami-cho, Tokyo. Issue:—son. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 93](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n93/mode/1up))

**Asaka** Prince Yasuhiko, 8th son of the late Prince Kuni and brother to the present head of the House, was b. '87: Capt., Infantry ; the House having been newly created on March 30th, '06: married with Princess Fumi, 8th daughter of the late Emperor Meiji, Aug. 7th, 1891. Res. Takanawa Nishi-daimachi, T. Issue:—a son and a daughter. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 93](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n93/mode/1up))

**Higashi-Kuni** Prince Naruhiko, Capt., Inf., 9th son of the late Prince Kuni and brother to the present head of the House, was b. '87 in Kyoto. Was ordered by the late Emperor on Nov. 3rd '06 to set up an independent house, has been betrothed to Princess Yasu, youngest sister to the Emperor. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 93](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n93/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Abe, Isoh** Prof. in the Waseda Univ., b. '65 in Fukuoka-ken ; studied in the Doshisha, Kyoto; then a pastor and afterward studied at Hartford College and at Glasgow ; Principal of the Doshisha Middle School, which he resigned and was for some years connected with several schools in Tokyo, finally engaged by the Waseda Univ., where he has charge of Athletic Association. Authority on social politics and wrote several books. Add. Zoshigaya, near Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Abe, Ko** Ex-Gov. of Tokyo, b. '48 in Morioka, Iwate-ken, and entered civil service early in Meiji era either at the head office or in the provinces; Director of Shrines and Temples Bureau '96-'99, mean while M.P. for his native place in '92; Governor of Chiba '99-'01. Governor of Niigata '01-'03, which he resigned to be nominated Member of the House of Peers, was again Governor of Niigata '05-'08 to be transferred to the present post in February '08, and retired Dec. '12. — p.  ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Abe, Taizo** b. in '49 at Toyohashi, Aichi-ken, entered Keiogijiku in '68; was its teacher for some years, then at the Imp. Univ., Tokyo, when he was 22 years old; visited U.S.A. in '77 under Viscount (then Mr.) Tanaka, Privy Councillor; resigned Gov. post and started the Meiji Life Insurance Company the following year '81; then founded ten years later the Meiji Fire Insurance Co., pioneer concerns in Japan. He is president of the two. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Abe, Teijiro** Maj.-Gen. Commander of Tsushima Garrison, was b 1861 in Niigata; Sub.-Lieut. of Engineering Corps '72, studied at Military Staff Col. '87-'89, then attached to the Staff Office, was Chief of staff of 2nd Depot Div. during the late Russo-Japan War, Com. of Japanese Garrison at Tientsin '07, then present post. Add. Tsushima. — p.  ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Aizo** Lt.-Gen. Commander of Third Field Artillery Brigade; was b. in '63 in Tokushima, artillery Sub.-Lieut. '83, student of the Staff Col. '88-'91, was Com. 11th Reg. during the Russo-Japan War, Chief Staff of 12 Division, then Commander of Garrison of Pescadores, Formosa, present post '14. — p.  ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Kenzo** M.P., was b. '64 in Kumamoto; Went to Korea and established several newspapers to promote the Japanese interest in the peninsula; made great efforts in regard to the organization of Kokken-to (political party); elected M.P. for five times; granted 4th class Merit of Rising Sun. Add. Yarai, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Kwan** Surg-Gen. (ret.), was b. 42 in Shizuoka, put on retired list in about 1902. Add. Higashi Gokencho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n98/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Adachi, Mine-ichiro** D.C.L. (Japan), Minister to Mexico, was b. '69 in Yamagata-ken, graduated law of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '92, attaché in '93, sent to Rome in '93, removed to Paris in '97, relieved of the post in '03 and attached to the head office, was in suite Peace Envoy to Portsmouth in '05, decorated with 3rd class Rising Sun with annuity in '06 for service in connection with the War. He was senior Councillor at head office before the transfer to Councillor of Paris Embassy '08, then the present post '13. Add. Japanese Legation, Mexico. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Taro** Dir. Iwaki Coll. Co. b. '51, in Choshu, Dir. of former Nippon Ry Co. '90-97, of former Seoul-Fuzan Ry Co. 04-07. Add. Kami Rokubancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Tsunayuki** ex-Inspector-General of the Metropolitan Police Board, b. in Satsuma, '59; filled post of chief Police Commissioner at several province; was appointed Director of the Police Bureau, Home Office in '01; the post of the Inspector-General he had to resign in Oct. '03 in consequence of the memorable anti-peace agitation Sept. 5th. Add. Nishi-kata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Aiba, Yosaburo** novelist, nom de plume Koson b. '55; is a prominent figure among the contemporary novelists, reputed for his light and delicate style; was connected for a while with the Yomiuri Shinbun, and now a regular contributor to the Tokyo Asahi. His chief works are Muratake, Kasano-Yuki, and some others. Add. Asahi Office, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Akaboshi, Tenta** Gov. of Kumamoto, b. '68 in Kumamoto, grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '96, on duty at District Taxation Offices '97-8, Councillor of Justice Dep't '00, Secretary of Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce '03; Judge Adm. Lit. Court '07; present post '13. Add. Kumamoto. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Akashi, Motojiro** Lieut.-Gen., Chief Police Commissioner and Com. of Gendarmes, Korean Residency-Gen, was b. at Fukuoka 1865, Sub.-Lieut. of Art. '83, studied at the Staff Coll. '87 and then in Germany '94-'95, staff officer of the Guards '95, was military attache to the French and the Russian Legations, held similar post at Berlin, '04-'05, Maj.-Gen. '07, Chief Staff to Korean Garrison '08, then transferred to the present post. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Akashi, Yoichiro** Dir. of Department of Education, was b. in Tokyo 1876; Grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '97, studied in France and Germany '02-'04, Personal Secretary to the Minister in addition to the Councillor '05, promoted to present post '10. Add. Haruki-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Akimoto, Okitomo** Viscount (cr.' 84), formerly Daimyo of Tatebayashi in the province of Kozuke, b. '57; Minister Resident and Minister Plenipotentiary from '90 to 92; is a leader of the Viscount coterie in the House of Peers, he being identified with the Seiyu-kai; a great connoisseur of pictorial art and a member of the Academy of Arts (London). Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Akita, Kiyoshi** Prop. of the Niroku (daily paper), M.P., Barrister; b. '81 in Tokushima; studied law in some private Univ.; passed the examination for the judicial service and appointed judge; gave up the profession and entered the Niroku. Add. Nirokusha, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Akitsuki, Satsuo** Amba'dor at Vienna since Dec. '09, b. '58 in Hyuga, graduated the former Law Col., of Justice Dep't in '84, studied at official expense in Belgium and Germany from '85 to '91; attaché in '91, Councillor in '92, transferred to Dep't Councillor, Consul at Fusan in '96; Legation Sec. at Seoul in the same year, transferred to Paris in '99, to St. Petersburg in '02, Minister Resident to Sweden-Norway in '04; Minister at Brussels in May '07; 3rd class Rising Sun in '07. Add. Japanese Embassy, Vienna. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n99/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Akiyama, Genzo** barrister in Tokyo, b. in Kakegawa, '59; graduated Law from the Imp. Univ., Tokyo '79; appointed a clerk at the Justice Dep't; next Judge at various local Courts; Chief of the Nagasaki District Court '87 and next of Yokohama District Court '92; Judge of the Supreme Court '98 which post he soon resigned to open law office in Tokyo and Yokohama. Add. Kogai-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Kōko** Lieut.-Gen. Com. of the 13th Army Div., was b. '56 in Ehime-ken, Sub-Lieut. in '79; Major-General in '99; Lieut.-General in '09; 2nd class Golden Kite in '09; Superintendent of Cavalry, the Military Education Board then present post '13. Add. Takata, Niigata. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Masanosuke** D. C. L. (Japan), b. '66 in Hiroshima-ken; graduated Law, Imp. Unv., of Tokyo in '86, to be connected afterward with civil affairs section of the War and Navy; removed to Seoul in '13. Add. Seoul. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Shinshi** Rear-Adm., staff officer at the Naval Headquarter Staff and instructor at the Naval Staff Coll., was b. in 1869 in Iyo, being brother to Lieut.-Gen. Akiyama. He graduated from the Naval Academy in 1886; is said to have been the most trusted adviser of Adm. Tōgo in the late war, when he was a Commander on board the Admiral's flagship Mikasa. Add. Shimoshibuya, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Teisuke** former prop. of the Niroku, and ex-M.P., b. at Kurashiki, Okayama-ken, '68, grad. Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo '09, earning his expenses by hard working; was appointed about 1892 junior auditor of the Board of Audit; started the Niroku in '93, which was suspended in a few years but revived '01; was elected a M.P. from Tokyo in the extraordinary election of '03 and '04, but was compelled to resign '05 owing to absurd charge of being a Russian spy. Add. Nishiki-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Amano, Tameyuki** Dr., D. C. L. (Japan), economist, Professor in Waseda University, b. in Sagaken, '59; grad. Tokyo University, '82; has devoted himself from that time to educational work at Waseda of which he is one of the "three main pillars"; sat at 1st and 2nd sessions in the House of Rep'tatives, but soon withdrew from the uncongenial atmosphere of practical politics. Wrote "Principles of Economic" and others; was edti. and prop. of the "Oriental Economist," a thrice-a-month journal of high authority and extensive circulation. Add. Iidamachi, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Ando, Jinsuke** Dir. of Furukawa Mining Co.; b. '54 in Shimane; entered early the Colonization Dept., Hokkaido, and studied mineralogy under an American expert; went to U. S. A. in '82; returning home he entered N. Y. K. and then the present Co. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Ando, Kenkichi** Mg. Dir. of the Nippon Elec. Light Co., mem. of the Tokyo City Assembly; b. '62 in Gifu-ken; studied in the Keio Univ.; passed examination Higher Civil service and for the Bar.; in '12 giving up the practice, entered business. Add. Nakaokachimachi, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n100/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Ando, Kensuke** ex-Gov. of Niigata b. '54 in Tosa; entered the diplomatic service; studied law in a Russian Univ., and was appointed Judge; in '96 appointed Gov. of Niigata, next transferred to Chiba and Nagasaki; once elected M.P. for Aichi-ken. Add. Niigata. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Ando, Taro** Pres. of Temperance Society, b. '46 in Tokyo, son of the physician to the Lord of Shima; joined Enomoto's pro-Tokugawa movement at Hakodate; was given office at the Foreign Office when released from imprisonment and was in the suite of the late Prince Iwakura's memorable envoy early in Meiji era. Consul at Hongkong, '74-'85, then at Shanghai '85 and Consul-General, '86-'89 at Honolulu where he was converted, returning home as fervent Christian and total abstainer. Subsequently held post of Director of Com. Bureau, Foreign Office, and of Commerce and Ind., Agri. and Com. Dep't. Add. Hommura cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Ando, Teibi** Baron (cr. '05) Commander of the Korean Garrison since 1912; was b. at Iida, Nagano-ken, in 1853; was educated at the now defunct Osaka Military School and was appointed Sub.-Lieut. '73; Maj. '83; Colonel '94; Maj.-Gen. '98; Lieut.-Gen. '05. In the Russo-Japanese war he at first commanded the 19th Brigade of Infantry and was soon made to lead the 10th Division. He assaulted the enemy's position at Wan-pao-shan in the memorable battle of Mukden. Add. Seoul Korea. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Ando, Yasutaro** Chief of the Electric Lighting Bureau, Tokyo City Office, was b. in 1863 in Yamanashi-ken. He was Dir. of the municipalized Tokyo Street Railway and is Auditor of the Kinugawa Hydro-electric Co. Add. Morigasaki, Ōmori, nr. T. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Anezaki, Masaji** Dr. Lit. (Japan), Prof. in Coll. of Literature, Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '72 in Kyoto; after grad. Philosophy at the Univ. in '95, studied in Germany for a few years; exchange prof. in Yale in 13. Add. Sasugayacho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Anraku, Kanemichi** ex-Chief Police Commissioner Tokyo, Mem. House of Peers, was b. '50 in Kagoshima; was connected with the Police service from about 1875, next held Governorship of Yamaguchi, Fukushima, Gifu, etc., resigned the Commissioner-ship in '08 when the Seiyu-kai Ministery fell, and entered commercial circle. Pres. Chiyoda Gas Co. till '11, and reappointed to the Chief Police Commissioner '11-'14. Retired the post at the fall of Saionji cabinet but reappointed in Feb. '13. Add. Yurakucho, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Aoki, Nobuzumi** Lieut.-Gen., and Com. of Port Arthur, was b. in 1859 in Hyuga; Sub.-Lieut. of Cavalry in 1879; studied in Belgium 1890-'93; Col. '02; Maj.-Gen. '07; has repeatedly been attached to the Japanese Legation at Peking, and is a noted Chinese expert. Add. Port Arthur. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Aoki, Tetsuji** Ph. D, Lawyer and patent attorney, was b. in 1875 in Mino, and was grad. from the Keio-gijuku '97 and further studied Law at Berlin and Gettingen 1899-'02; passed the Examination for the Bench in '98, and was a probationary Judge at Kobe for a few months. Despatched by the Alma mater to Germany to study Commercial Law '99-'02; Prof. at the Keio; left it in '08, and opened law office. Add. Minami-Nabe-cho, Ky., T. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Aoki, Tetsutaro** Gov. Commercial Agent in London since '10-'12, was b. Okayama 1867; had long been connected with Yokohama Specie Bank and was its London Agent for many years still about 1904. Add. Meguro, near Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n101/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Aoyama, Tanemichi** M.D. (Japan), Prof. of Medicine at the Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '59; studied medicine in Germany. He was almost killed by pest at Hongong in '94 where he with Dr. Kitazato was despatched by the Government to investigate the disease, and by which he was affected. He is one of most distinguished experts in internal diseases, and is physician-in-ordinary to the Court. Add. Yumi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Aoyama, Yukinobu** Visc. Mem. House of Peers, Dir. of Tamagawa Tramway Co. etc., former daimyo of Yawata, Mino; b. '54. Add. Kobikicho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Arai, Kentaro** Dir. of Korean Residency-Gen., Finance Dept.; b. '63 in Niigata and grad. Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo, '90, after which he was connected with the Treasury in which he rose to the position of the Vice-Minister in '06; removed to the present position in '07. Add. Iida-machi, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Arai, Taiji** Mem. House of Peers, director of various commercial establishments in Formosa; b. '61 in Sendai; studied under Nakae Chomin, the Rousseau of Japan; assisted late Mr. Tomita in the establishment of the Bank of Japan; then presidents of Kanegafuchi Cotton Spinning Co. etc.; elected representative of the Highest Tax Payers for Miyagi '99. Add. Yedogawa, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Arakawa, Gitaro** Mayor of Yokohama 10-13, b. Tokyo in '62; graduated Col. of Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo in '84, to enter civil service in the Home Office. Was Councillor at the head office and next Secretary at Toyama, Gumma, Kanagawa; Governor of Tottori, '97, Miye '99, Kagawa, 1900, and Nagasaki '08-10. Add. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Arakawa, Minoji** Minister to Madrid (app. 1909), b. '57 in Kagoshima, grad. Eng'ing Col., Tokyo, in '80, clerk at the Legation, London, in '86, attaché in '87, élève Consul at Tientsin in '90, at Ninsen, '94, attached to the 2nd Army during the Japan-China War, Consul at Tientsin in '95, at Suchow in '96 Consul at London in '96 where he was promoted to Consul-General, decorated with 3rd Class Rising Sun with grant of money for the war. Add. Japanese Legation, Madrid. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Araki, Kwampo** Mem. of Society of Arts, London; b. '31 in Kochiken, one of the most distinguished painters of the Shijo school; studied the art under the celebrated Araki Kwansai who ultimately adopted him; was appointed Painter of the fief of Tosa. After the Restoration, he taught painting at Peerage Girls' and other schools, and was recently a Professor at the Gov. Fine Arts Institute, '98-'06; was appointed 1900 a member of the Fine Arts Committee attached to the Imp. Household. Add. Nishimachi, Shitaya, T. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Arichi, Shinanosuke** Baron, (cr. '00), Vice-Admiral (retired), Mem. House of Peers, was b. in Choshu, '43; Lieut.-Commander, '72; Commander of Yokosuka Admiralty and Kure; then successively Chief of Naval Staff; Commander-in-Chief of Standing Fleet, &c.. is at present devoting himself to promoting the cause of the Japan Maritime Society of which he is president. Add. Awaji-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n102/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Ariga, Nagabumi** Manager of the Mitsui family affairs, b. '65 in Osaka-fu, and younger brother to Dr. Nagao Ariga; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '89; was in Government service for a long while, ultimately rising to be a Bureau Director in Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce about '02. Add. Iigura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Ariga, Nagao, Dr., D.C.L. & D. Lit. (Japan)** b. in Settsu, '60; grad. Tokyo University, '81; Private Secretary to Prince Ito (then President of Privy Council), '87; afterwards Councillor at the Department of Agriculture and Commerce; took charge of affairs relating to international law during the Japan-China War of '94-'95; and held a similar post in the late campaign. He is a Professor at Waseda University, and has represented the Japan Red Cross Society at several international conferences, the late in London, '07. Has written several works on history, diplomacy and international law; 2nd Class Rising Sun, and was a recipient of the Imp. Japanese Academy prize in '12. Adviser to China to draw up Constitution in '13. Add. Myogadani, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Arima, Ryokitsu** Vice-Adm., Pres. of the Naval Academy since '14 and hero of the Port Arthur blockading operation in the late war, was b. at Wakayama 1862; midshipman '88, successively held posts of Captain of cr. Kasagi, chief staff of Takeshiki Naval Port, Captain of cr. Iwate, Dir. Naval Artillery School etc., appointed Vice-Adm. '13 and member of the Adm. Council: Dec. '10, having been promoted to Rear-Adm. '09; Com. of 1st Squadron '12. Add. Etajima, Hiroshima. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Arima, Sumibumi** Viscount, Sub.-Lieut. Cavalry (ret.), aide-de-camp to the Crown Prince, formerly of the fief of Maruoka, Echizen; was b. 1863, eldest son of the former Lord of the fief; Grad. Agr. Chemistry, Agr. Col., Tokyo, '92; one-year volunteer in '97, appointed the aid-de-camp '10, Lord Steward to Prince Kuni '10. Add. Ukyo-machi, Y., Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Arima, Yorikazu** Count, formerly Daimyo of the Kurume clan, 5th son and heir to his predecessor, b. '64, served in the Imperial Court for years as Chamberlain. Add. Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Arimatsu, Hideyoshi** Chief Sec. to the Privy Council, was born at Okayama, '63; Director of Police Bureau of Home Dep't., '93; Gov. of Miye Prefec., '04; Director of Police Bureau, '08; Dir. of Imp. Foresty Bureau, '11-'14; present post, April '14. Add. 2, Nakasatocho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Ario, Keiju** Man. of the Hypothec Bank of Japan, was b. in Mino in '49, was once an official of the Treasury, then entered banking circle and was appointed to present post since its foundations. Add. Kami-fujimai, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Arisaka, Nariaki** Lieut.-General, Baron (cr. '07), inventor of the Arisaka-gun, b. '52 in Choshu; was first a teacher in foreign languages, connected with a military cadet school; transferred to an Arsenal and to an Artillery head-quarter and finally appointed Captain (art.), '81; sent abroad about '92; Col. '95; completed a new type of quick-firing mountain gun in Nov. '01 after six years' persistent effort, meanwhile again sent abroad '99 on official business. He was decorated with 2nd Class Rising Sun and grant of yen 5,000 for his invention, and was promoted Major-Gen., '01. Was granted 2nd Class Golden Kite (war honour). Placed on the reserve list, Jan. '13. Add. Tsukudo-Hachiman-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n103/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Arisaka, Shozo** Inspector of Naval Ordinance and Prof. at the Imperial University Tokyo ; b. in '67 in Tokyo ; graduated his special course at the University in '90 ; sent by the Navy to France where he studied at Hotchkiss and other works from '91 to '93 ; appointed expert to the Navy on his return and was steadily promoted, mean-while devising a new plug for 12th quickfiers ; has been sent twice to England on official business. Add. Imp. Univ. of Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Arishima, Takeshi** Aud., Nippon Yusen Kaisha, b. '42 at Kagoshima ; took active part in the War of Restoration ; Dir. Tax Bureau, '72 ; sent to Europe and America, '78 ; Customs Chief, Yokohama, '82 ; Dir. Customs Bureau, '91 which he resigned '93 to take charge of financial affairs of the great house of Shimazu. Add. Bancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Ariyoshi, Tadaichi** Gov. of Miyazaki was b. '70 in Tango, grad. Law, at Imp. Tokyo University in 96, Sec. Home Office till '08, Governor of Chiba-ken '08-'10, transferred to Dir. General Affairs, Korean Res.-Gen. in '10, transferred to the present post in '11. Add. Miyazaki Prefectural Office. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Ariyoshi, Eiji** Director of the Forest Bureau, Household ; was b. '93 in Okayama, grad. '88 from the German Language School, Tokyo and passed the higher civil service examination the following year, was in the Justice Dep't till 1893; Councillor of the Dep't of Agr. and Commerce, then of Home Office and of the Legislative Bureau from '94 to '08, attended Inter'nal Sen'ry Conference held at Madrid and similar conference for ex-convicts at Antwerps in '98; transferred to the Police Bureau the same year; again Councillor of the Legislative Bureau in '02; Dir. of Police Bureau in '03; Governor of Miye-ken '04, transferred to the Police Bureau in '08, then to the present post '11. Add. Nakazato, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Asabuki, Yeiji** Ex-Director of the Mitsui Company ; b. '49 in Oita-ken, was in youth a hot-headed anti-foreign fanatic, and came up to Tokyo to make attempt on the late Fukuzawa, a pioner advocate of foreign learning, to be soon converted by him into one of his eager disciples, as even to marry a daughter of his elder sister. After grad. the Keiogijuku, he entered the Mitsubishi ; started direct export business of raw silk in which he failed. Next he entered the Mitsui and pushed spinning business (Kanegafuchi Sp. Mill) to the present state of prosperity. Add. Kobiki-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Asada, Eiji** Ph. D., Prof. Foreign Language School, was b. in Tokyo in 1865, graduated from Northwestern Univ. and next Chicago Univ., returning home in '97 ; is also a text-book commissioner, Department of Education. Add. Kogaicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Asada, Nobuoki** Baron (cr. '07), 2nd Class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honour), Gen., Superintendent of Education Board since '12; b. at Kawagoye (near Tokyo), '48 ; grad. Mil. Academy at Osaka, '73 and commissioned ensign : fought in the civil War of '77 as Capt. (local rank) and decorated with 5th Order ; Maj. '84, Lieut.-Col. '87 ; Col. 94; Major-Gen. '97 and Chief Staff of Eastern Military Section ; Com. of a Brigade of various Divisions; took active part as Brig. Com. at Fenshuiling, and succeeded Gen. Hasegawa after Liaoyang as Com. of the Guards Division; promoted to full Gen. 12. Add. Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Asada, Yasunori** Mem. House of Peers, b. in Kyoto, 48 ; First Class Councillor to Japanese Legation at Washington, U.S.A. '74 ; Councillor of the Department for Foreign Affairs, '79; Director of the Bureau of Commercial Affairs, '86; Governor of Nagano-ken and next of Kanagawa-ken, '99; Vice-Minister for foreign Affairs, '00; then that of Communications, which he resigned, '02. Add. Shimo-6-bancho, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n104/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Asahina, Chisen** journalist b. '62 in Mito; left '83 the Imperial Tokyo University, law, unfinished on account of ill health; shortly afterward joined the staff of the Nichi Nichi, when Viscount Ito (Miyoji) was proprietor, and was its editor for many years. Went twice to Europe; left the Nichi Nichi about 1905 when the proprietor changed, and is at present a journalistic free lance. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Asakawa, Toshiyasu** Maj.-Gen., Superintendent of the Horse Adm. Bureau, b. 1860 in Yamanashi, grad. Mil. College '86, Capt. '98; Col. '04 and present rank '09; was awarded 3rd Class Golden Kite in connection with the late war. Add. Okubo nr. Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Asano, Nagakoto** Marquis; b. '42, and is chief of the former clan of Hiroshima with 426,000 koku which was founded by Nagamasa (1546-1610 A.D.), one of the great generals of Hideyoshi; was accredited to Rome, 1882-'84. Add. Yayoi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Asano, O-suke** Dr. E., chief electrician of the Department of Communications, b. in '59 in Okayama; graduate electricity at the Government Engineering College in '81, since then he has chiefly been connected with that Department, meanwhile despatched to Europe and America two or three times. Add. Kogai-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Asano, Soichiro** President of the Toyo Steamship Co, and Dr. of many other concerns, was b. '48 in Etchu (Toyama-ken). He arrived in Tokyo '71 with empty purse; proceeded to Yokohama supporting himself by hawking cheap viands; bought from the Yokohama Gas Works coaltar and other refuses then left unutilized, and laid the foundation of the present fortune. In '75 he brought the Gov. Cement Works at Fukagawa and began to produce "Asano Cement" now widely known; started tank oil business, '93 and promoted with others the Toyo S.S. Co, '95. Add. Shiba Tamachi, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Atomi, Takino** b. in '40 at Osaka, one of the distinguished lady painters and educators; founder and proprietor of the Atomi Girls' School. Visited U.S.A. on the occasion of St Louis Fair. Add. Yanagi-cho, Koi., T. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Awazu, Seiryo** D. C. L. (Japan), Prof. of Imp. Tokyo Univ., Waseda Univ., etc., insurance expert, was b. at Kyoto in '71, grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '94, and has since been engaged in insurance business at the Department of Agriculture and Commerce, as organizer of a number of life insurance cos., besides undertaking teaching. Attended the World's Insurance Congress held at New York '03. Add. Misaki-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Ayai, Tadahiko** Business Manager of the Murai Bank; b. '71 in Oita-ken; grad. Post and Telegram School in Tokyo; served Communication Dept. for years; went to U.S.A. to accomplish his study in '90; after returning home he joined the Murai Bros. & Co. and then rose to the present post. Add. Sanya, Yoyogi, near Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Baba, Genji** Judge of Court of Cassation, b. '60; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. of Tokyo, class '85; to be appointed a junior judge in Tokyo Court and Judge of Supreme Court, and ultimately rising to the present post in Apr. '13. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n105/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Baba, Michihisa** b. '47 at Iwase, Toyama-ken, of a family of shipowner, is Pres. Iwase Bank and Dir. Tokyo Marine Ins. Co., Imp. Marine Ins. Co., Innoshima Dockyard, and others. Is a millionaire in Toyama and one of the wealthiest individual ship-owners in Japan. Add. Torinichome, Nih., T. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Banno, Otoya** Dir. of the Japan Industrial Bank. was born '67 in Tokyo; graduated in '92 from the Law Col., Imp. Tokyo University under adverse circumstances; then served at the Financial Department, but soon resigned to enter the present bank on its establishment. Add. Kaga-cho, U., T. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Chikami, Kiyo-omi** Mem. House of Peers, was b. '56 in Kochi-ken and finished Lit. Course, Tokyo Univ. about '82; further studied in England and after returning home about 1887 he was appointed instructor at the Tokyo High School, Kagoskima Zoshikan, Sendai High School, etc.; was appointed Councillor of the Home Office, '96, Governor of Niigata, then of Kagoshima till '09 Add. Nibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Chinda, Sutemi** Viscount (Baron '07, Viscount '11), Ambassador to Washington; b. at Hirosaki, '56; graduated an American University '81; appointed a clerk of the Foreign Office, '85 and had charge of the Telegraph Section in the same office; appointed Consul at San Francisco, '90; removed to the Consulate at Chemulpo; the Consul-Generalship at Shanghai, '95 Minister Resident at Brazil, next at the Hague, '99-1900; Minister Plenipotentiary and Envoy Extraordinary at St. Petersburg, 1900; recalled in '01 by the then Foreign Min. Baron Komura to act as his immediate subordinate, and had to take charge of the Office while his superior was away to Portsmouth and Peking. In recognition of this special service he was invested with 1st Class Sacred Treasure and also raised to Peerage. Amb. to Court of Berlin '08 then present post in Nov. 1911. Add. Japanese Embassy, Washington. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Chisaka, Chijiro** Rear. Adm. and Chief of Staff of the Sasebo Admiralty, was b. 1864 in Yonezawa; midshipman '85; Commander and Adjutant of Fleet Adm. Ito, '95-'98; Capt. and Com. of the Sasebo Torpedo Corps 1910; present post 14. Add. Sasebo. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Date, Munenobu** Marquis, b. '60, son of Munenori who took a distinguished part in upholding the cause of the Restoration and who, on that account, was afterward granted a higher grade of nobility than that of the main house at Sendai which was a tardy supporter of the Imperialist cause at that time, this scion having held fief of only 100,000 koku at Uwajima, Iyo. Add. Takanawa, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Dan, Takuma** Director of Mitsui Bank, Mitsui Mining Co., &c.; b. '58 at Fukuoka, was sent for study to America early in Meiji era by the Lord of Fukuoka and completed the course of mining at the Boston Tech. Institute, U.S.A., and was for some years teacher of English at Gov. school, technical official at the Gov. Meteorological Observatory and next at the Miike Coalmine, with the purchase of which by the Mitsui he entered the service of the Firm. Add. Tango-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Den, Kenjiro** Baron (cr. '07), Member of the House of Peers and ex-Vice-Minister of Communications, b. '55 in Hyogo-ken; held at several places post of local Chief Police Inspector; Vice-Min. of Communications, Jan.-Jun. '98; President of the Kansai R'ly Co., at the same time M.P for Hyogo-ken; was again Vice-Min. of Communications, '00-'06. He is now a prominent figure in business circles. Add. Kanasugi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 61 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n106/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Dewa, Shigeto** Admiral, Baron (cr. '07), holder of 2nd Class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of the Rising Sun; b. at Aizu; grad. the Naval Academy in '82; was Capt. of cru. Takachiho in the Japan-China war 1896-'95; chief of committee to bring home Tokiwa from England '99; Rear-Adm. 1900 and commanded the Standing Squadron at the time of the Boxer's trouble; afterward transferred to Chief Construction Board at Yokosuka, next to Director of Naval Affairs Bureau; commanded a Squadron in the late war. Com. of 1st Squadron since Feb. '11. Add. Sasebo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Dohi, Keizo** Dr., Prof. of the Dermatological Dept. of Med. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Chairman of the Japanese Dermatological Society; grad. the Med. Col. '91; assist. in the surgical clinic and student of the Univ. Hall at his alma mater; studied his speciality in Austria, Germany, and France, '04-'98; appointed to the present situation on his returning home '98; was on suite of the late Prince Komatsu when His Highness attended the Coronation Ceremony of King Edward, '02. Corresponding member of the Vienese ('02), Berliner ('03,) and Parisian Dermatological Society ('11,) recommended to the honorable member of the Italian Dermatological Society '06; Editor of the Japanese Journal of Dermatology & urlogy. Add. Shimo Nibancho, K., T. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Doi, Michio** Chairman of the Osaka Chamber of the Commerce, b. in Kanagawa, '41; was a Judge till '84 when he entered the service of the Konoike family, Osaka, of which he is now an adviser. Visited U.S.A. in '09 as one of the Business men's party invited by American Chamber of Commerce. Add. Konoike, Osaka. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Ebara, Soroku** ex.-M.P., educationalist, a leader of the Seiyu-kai, Member of the House of Peers '12; b. at Tokyo in '42; was first a military officer, but subsequently resigned the commission and was for somewhile Secretary of the Prefecture of Shizuoka, '70. He is chiefly known in connection with subsequent non-official career: has sat several time in the House where he lately represented Tokyo. Is Director of Azabu Middle School. Mem. of the Higher Education Council and is a devout Christian. Add. Torii-zaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Ebina, Danjo** Pres. of Aoyama Gakuin; b. '56 in Yanagawa; studied at the Foreign Language School in Kumamoto; then theology at the Doshisha. He has subsequently led an active life in evangelistic and educational word, having established a number of churches and schools, being at present pastor of the Hongo Church, Tokyo. Add. Hayashi-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Egi, Chu** Barrister, b. '58, brother to Senshi Egi; grad. Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '84; he practised his profession, giving lectures on Criminal Law in the Chuo Univ. all the while; an Official of the Judicial Office and of the Foreign Office; again resumed his former profession. Has written "Principles of Criminal Law," and is a noted satirist. Add. Awajicho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Egi, Senshi** Mem. House of Peers, Lord in Waiting of Kinkei Hall, brother of above, b. '53, studied Law at Tokyo Gov. Univ. and was in service of Education Dep't. '72-'77, transferred to Home Office in which he Dir. of Local Adm. Bureau '90-'96, was next Governor of Ibaraki, Aichi, Hiroshima and Kumamoto till '06 when he resigned on account of illness. Add. Yocho-machi, Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n107/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Egi, Yoku** Chief-Sec. of the Cabinet was b. '73; adopted son and heir of the above; Sec. Chief of Colonial Bureau, 1910-'13, it was abolished; appointed Sec. Chief of 2nd Okuma Cabinet, 1914. Add. Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Eitaki, Hisakichi** Consul-Gen. at Honolulu since '12, was b. '66 in Echigo, passed legal service examination in '90 appointed Junior Judge in '93 and transferred to the Foreign Office the same year, first appointed Secretary at Consulate, Fusan, in '93 to be promoted to élève Consul the same year, was Consul at Ninsen, Shanghai, Shashi, Sydney and Hankow between '94 and '05, Consul-General at Shanghai between '05, and '09, then at Chientao till '12. Add. Japanese Con.-Gen. Honolulu. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Ema, Shunichi** Barrister, ex-M.P., a Mem. of the Tokyo Municipality, b. in '61, in Totomi, 3rd son of Nanyo Yamamoto, and adopted by Ema Toguro. Early came up to Tokyo to study politics; was admitted to the Bar in '93 and afterwards elected Pres. of the Tokyo Barrister's Association; was elected Vice-Pres. of Tokyo Prefectural Assembly '89. Add. Kuromon-cho, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Eto, Yasutaro** Rear.-Adm., Chief Staff Officer of the Sasebo Admiralty, b. '95 in Saga-ken; early grad. Naval Academy and appointed Sub.-Lieut. of the Navy; Staff Officer of the Naval Staff Board at the time of the Russo-Japanese War, for which he was granted 3rd class of Golden Kite; Com. of the newly built Ikoma, for a while; Rear-Adm. '07 then at Port Arthur Adm. Add. Sasebo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fujii, Ko-ichi** Vice-Adm., Com. of Sasebo Admiralty since '14 and ex-Deputy Chief of the General Staff; b. '57 in Okayama-ken; Sub-Lieut. '83 to be rapidly promoted to the present rank, filling during the interval posts of Vice-Com. of the Chin-en, Staff of the Sasebo and Yokosuka Adm'tries, Com. of the Suma and of Akitsushima, Attaché to the Japanese Legation, Berlin, etc. Vice-Adm. in '09 with transper to present post. Add. Sasebo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fujii, Shigeta** Lieut.-Gen., Commander of Kokura Division; b. '60 in Hyogo-ken; Ensign of Artillery in '79; entered the Military Staff Col. in '84; studied tactics in Germany in '90; Staff Officer to the 2nd Army in the Japan-China War; Major-Gen., '02 and Dir. Military Staff College, Chief Staff to Gen. Kuroki's Army the late war, Dir. of Art. and Engineering School, '06, Com. of Tokyo Bay Forts 09-11; transferred to the present position in '11. Add. Oban-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fujimura, Yoshiro** Baron, Bar, of Mid. Tem. was b. in Kumamoto in '70, proceeded to England after finishing secondary education in Japan, and grad. Law at Cambridge, returning home '03 when he entered the Mitsui, and was its London agent till '10, then removed to the head office. Add. Akashi-cho, Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fijimuro, Matsujiro** Maj.-Gen. of Artillery (ret.), was b. in 1863 Hiroshima, grad. from the Staff College in '96 studied in Russia '97-'00, was attached to the Central Staff then a Reg. Commander, and Chief Staff of the Guards Division till '08, Maj.-Gen. '10. Add. Kogai-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n108/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Fujinami, Kototada** Viscount (cr. '84), b. at Kyoto '53; Chamberlain to late Emperor from childhood, and since '80 Superintendent of the Imperial Mews, Deputy Pre. of Horse Adm. Bureau which he has resigned. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fujioka, Ichisuke** Dr. Eng. (Japan) was b. in Choshu, '56; grad. from Tokyo Engineering College in '81; appointed its Professor from '84 to '86 when he retired to start electric business; chief electrician to the Tokyo Electric Light Co. in '92 and founder of the Tokyo Street Railway Co. which with two other street railway co.'s was fused '06; he has been to Europe and America four or five times for study, on business etc. and was under Edison for somewhile. Add. Zaimoku-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fujisawa, Rikitaro** Dr. Sc. (Japan) Prof. of Mathematics at Tokyo University, b. in Echigo (Niigata-ken), '61; pursued his special study in England and Germany, '83-'87. He has written several works on Mathematics. Add. Suwa-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Heitaro** 2nd Baron, one of the millionaires in Osaka, b. in Osaka, '69; son of late Denzaburo Fujita who started in Osaka the Fujita Firm to undertake contract business with the Government, beside undertaking mining and engineering works. Educated at Cambridge Univ. Add. Amijima, Kita-ku, Osaka. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Koriki** Dir. of South Manchuria Rly since 1914, ex-Administration Dept. Imp. R'ly Board 13-14, was b. '66 in Tottori, grad. Law at the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo in '99; President of a local school the same year; transferred to the Dep't of Communications in '93, and finally to the present post in May '14. Has been twice despatched abroad on official business. Add. Dairen. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Shiro** Member of the House of Peers, b. at Tokyo, '61; attaché to Japanese Legation in Vienna, '86, then in Berlin; afterwards Private Secretary to the Minister of Communications and of Agriculture and Commerce, &c.; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, '98; now President of the Nippon Fire Insurance Co. and of other concerns. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Taka-aburo** ex-President and Chief Judge of the Nagoya Appeal Court, b. '56 at Uwajima, Iyo; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '78; Judge in '81; for sometime a jun. Sec. and Vice-Dir. Commercial Bureau, Foreign Office; returned to the Bench and continues to occupy the post from '98; relieved of the post in Apr. '13. Add. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fujiyama, Raita** President of the Nippon Sugar Refinery Co., b. in '65 in Karatsu; grad. Keiogijuku in '90; then entered the Mitsui after the notorious scandal culminating in the suicide of the former president. Add. Shirokane, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fukai, Eigo** Bureau Dir. the Nippon Ginko, b. 71 in Gumma-ken; on the staff of the Kokumin Shimbun 1898-'05; Private Secretary to Gov. of the Bank 06-12. Add. Aoyama, Rokuchome, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fukuda, Tokuzo** D.C.L. (Japan) Prof. in the Keio Gijuku Univ., b. '74 in Tokyo, being the 1st son of Tokubei Fukuda; graduated from the Tokyo Higher Commercial School, class '94; was dispatched by the Educational Dep't. to Germany, France, and Italy to make further study on Economics; Prof. at his Alma Mater on his return home, but resigned '06; wrote several works on Economics. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n109/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Fukuda, Umanosuke** Inspector-Gen. of Naval Construction, Sectional Chief of the Naval Dep't. of Materials and Yokosuka Naval Yard, was b. '56 in Aichi-ken; Lieut.-Constructor in '88 and promoted to the present rank some time ago; served meanwhile as Mem. of the Naval Board for Materials, and as Chief of the Construction Dep't. of Maidzuru Admiralty. Is the Holder of 4th class of Golden Kite (War honour). Add. Nakano, nr. Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Fukuhara, Arinobu** Pres. of the Aikoku Life Insurance Co., b. '48 in Awa (Chiba-ken); started in '72 the Shiseido drug store, one of the leading establishments in Tokyo in this line; promoted with others the above company and has since been chairman of Board of Directors. Travelled through Europe and U.S.A. several years ago to inspect the insurance affairs there. Add. Izumo-cho, Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Fukuhara, Ryojiro** Vice-Minister, Education and Commissioner of the Imp. Household since '07, b. '69 in Ise, graduated Law Imp. Univ., Tokyo '92. Held various junior posts at central Government and then transferred to local Offices as Councillor or Chief Police Commissioner; removed to the Education Dep't., '98 as Councillor and Inspector; dispatched to Europe '99 to investigate affairs of educational administration, and returning home '02 was made Councillor and Section Chief of the Department; the Director in '04, and the Vice-Minister in '11. Add. Minami-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Fukui, Kikusaburo** Mng. Dir. of the Mitsui Bussan, was b. in 1866 in Tokyo, grad. the Tokyo Higher Commercial School in '83 and then entered the service of the Mitsui, to be attached to the Branches at Shanghai, Singapore, Hongkong, Osaka, etc., and then the agent at New York for several years. Add. Mitsui & Co., Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Fukuoka, Takayoshi** Viscount (cr. '84), Privy Councillor, b. in Tosa, '35; after the Restoration of '97 entered civil service and became Minister of Education, '81-'82. Appointed in '88 to the present post. Add. Kanatomi-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Fukushima, Namizo** head of Fukushima & Co., a leading firm in the line of Bonds, Shares, etc., Trust & Financial Agents, was born in 1860. He started as a Stock Exchange Broker in 1887, and after a stormy life for twenty years, has risen to the point. He had retired from the membership of the Tokyo Stock Exchange, to devote himself to negotiable securities business both as home and foreign markets. This kind of business had been formerly in the hand of foreign firm in Japan, but he is now a foremost Japanese in this special line, pushing it with unusual vigor and activity. Add. Kabuto-cho, Nihonbashi, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Fukushima, Yasumasa** Baron (cr. '07), holder of 2nd Class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor), Lieut.-General, Governor-General of Kwantung, was born in Matsumoto, Nagano-ken, 53; Lieutenant '78; Captain '83; Military Attaché to Japanese Legation at Berlin '87-'92; relieved and thence he returned home in June 1893 via Siberia on horseback; Major-General in '00, and acquired international fame as Commander of the Japanese contingent on the occasion of the Boxer trouble, and was knighted by the late King Edward. Was on the Staff of Marshal Oyama in the late War in which he lost his son. Appointed the Vice-Chief of the General Staff Office in '06, then to the present post in '12. Add. Port Arthur. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n110/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Fukuzawa, Ichitaro** Prof. at the Keio-gijnku, b. Dec. '63, eldest son of the late Y. Fukuzawa; educated at Yale Univ. after he finished the Keio. Add. Mita, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Fukuzawa, Momosuke** M.P., brother-in-law of the Fukuzawa brothers being the husband of their sister, b. in '64 at Kawagoye; studied at Keio-gijuku; and proceeded to U. S. A. after grad. from that institution and studied at a Commercial Col. there. Returning home '89, he first entered Tokyo Ice Works, Oji Paper Mill and others, and finally the Hokkaido Colliery and Steamship Co., of which he was Agent for Tokyo, besides sitting on the boards of directors of several other concerns. Add. Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Fukuzawa, Sutejiro** born in Tokyo '63, second son of the late celebrated Fukuzawa; was together with his eldest brother Mr. Ichitaro Fukuzawa, entered Yale Univ., '83, the younger Fukuzawa to pursue engineering and the elder literature. They both returned home in '90, and Mr. Sutejiro has since been managing the Jiji and the Osaka Jiji, the latter started '05. Married in '90 Kiku, eldest daughter of Count Hayashi, ex-Min. of Foreign Affairs; 2 sons and 3 daughters. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Funakoshi, Mitsunojo** Councillor of Embassy at Berlin; b. '67 in Hiroshima, passed the diplomatic and consular service examination in '94, élève Consul at Vladivostock the same year, at Fusan and then at San Francisco in '95, Legation Sec. at Washington in '97, transferred to London in '99, Secretary at head office '02, Sec. at Vienna in '03, Sec. at Berlin '06-'09, Chief of Cor. Sec., For. Office and then present post in '12. Studied in Germany '86-'93. Add. Japanese Embassy, Berlin. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Furuichi, Koi** Dr. Eng. (Japan), Member of the House of Peers, was born at Himeji, '54; pursued his engineering study in France '75-'80; filled the posts of the Directorship of the Public Works Bureau, Home Office; Vice-Minister of Communications, and Director-General of the Government Railways, which he resigned in '03 in order to take up the post of Directorship of Seoul-Fusan R'ly which he resigned, '07. He is one of the best amateur no singer and actors. Add. Yumi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Furukawa, Sakajiro** Vice-Pres. of the Imp. R'ly since '14, b. '58 in Yamaguchi; graduated Engineering in the former Engineering College in '80; next entered the Gov. R'ly Bureau as its Engineer; was connected with the field r'ly during the late war, transferred to the old post in '08; Chief of Central District in '09, Eng.-in-Chief in '13. Add. Hirakawa-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Furukawa, Toranosuke** President of the Furukawa Mining Co., son of the late celebrated mine-owner Ichibei Furukawa who adopted Jyunkichi, 2nd son of late Count Mutsu, as his heir, but the latter dying in '07, Toranosuke become the head of the family so that he inherited his father's immense properties including more than twenty mines. He was b. in '82 and graduated Mining at Columbia, U.S.A., class '07; was granted 3rd class Treasure, 1911, for his munificent gifts to various schools. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Furuya, Hisatsuna** Master of Ceremonies & Steward to Prince Yi-kong of Chosen; b. '74 in Iyo; studied at Brussels after he finished Doshisha College; Priv. Sec. to late Prince Ito 1900, Sec. Imp. Household '02; Priv. Sec. to late Prince Ito '01-'09 and accompanied him to Harbin on the fatal tour; then the present post. Add. Hinokicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n111/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Ga, Reishi** Member of the House of Peers since 1891, b. at Nagasaki in '40; a pioneer scholar of Dutch; held several important posts in the Government service. Add. Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Gejo, Masao** Paymaster-Inspector of the Navy (ret.), Mem. of House of Peers, was b. in Yonezawa '42. Began his career in the Navy as jun.-clerk '73 then successively held posts of Sectional Chief of Superintendence for Accounts of Yokosuka Admiralty, Chief Paymaster of Saseho Adm. and Pres. of the Naval Paymaster's College. He is a noted amateur painter and a connoisseur. Add. Shimo-6-ban-cho, Kojimachi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Go, Seinosuke** Baron. Mem. of House of Peers, President of Teikoku Shyogo Ginko, Tokyo Stock Exchange and others; b. '66 in Tokyo, son of late Junzo Go; learned in Europe; after several Govt. services he entered business circle. Add. Hashiba, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Goto, Shimpei** Baron (cr. '06), ex-Minister of Communications and President of Imperial Railways, b. '56 in Iwate-ken, son of a doctor, and is a nephew of the celebrated patriot Takano who was prosecuted to death by the Tokugawa Government a little before the Restoration. He studied medicine both in Japan and Germany; was chief of the Nagoya Hospital. Subsequently appointed junior Director of the Sanitary Bureau, Home Office; was arrested on charge of implicity in the notorious Soma scandal to be acquitted about a year afterward. He got appointment as Sanitary Commissioner at the time of the Japan-China War, after which he re-entered the Sanitary Bureau as its Director. Was singled out '97 by the late Vis. Kodama, newly appointed Governor-General of Formosa, as Director of the Civil Administration Bureau which he had continued to occupy till his transfer to the Presidency of South Manchuria Railway in '06, and then the Ministerial chair in '08. Once combined the office of Deputy-Pre. of the Colonial Bureau, Cabinet, cr. '10. Retired from the posts in Aug. '11 and again held them in the 3rd Katsura Cabinet; formed the Doshikai with late Prince Katsura, but left it in 1913. Add. Miyamura-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Gwassan, Yagoro** master sword-smith appointed '06 Art Commissioner to Imp. Household, b. '46 in Omi and learned craft under Teikichi Gwassan at Osaka by whom he was ultimately adopted as son and heir; is the greatest master in producing swords of Soshu and Ichimonji style, besides creating a new mode of tempering. He has made swords for the late Emperor and the Crown Prince. Add. Yariya-cho, Osaka. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Hachisuka, Shigeakira** Marq., (cr. 1884; formerly daimyo of Tokushima), a Privy Councillor, b. '46; represented Japan in France about '81; was appointed President of the House of Peers '91-'96; Minister of Education, Sept. '96-Nov. '97; was appointed to the present post following year. Add. Mita-tsuna-machi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Haga, Yaichi** Dr. Lit. (Japan), Prof. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ., was b. '63 in Fukui-ken; grad. Lit. Col. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '89 to study at the University Hall afterward; appointed Professor at the 1st Higher School, and that at the Higher Normal School; Assis. Prof. in his alma master in '98 and full Prof. in the following year; then transferred to the Higher Normal School; sent to Germany to prosecute further study; was given a chair in the University when he returned home in '02. Add. Otowa, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n112/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Hama, Koichi** Departmental Chief of the Board of Audit, was b. in '48 entered official service first as a junior official in the Board of the Financial Dep't. to be afterwards promoted to the present post. Add. Otsuka Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hamada, Gentatsu** Dr. M. (Japan) b. '54 in Kumamoto-ken; studied medicine in Germany '85-'88; Professor of Medical Course in the Tokyo Univ., '89-1900; is an acknowledged authority on women's diseases, and is the chief and proprietor of the Hamada Hospital for women's diseases, a physician-in-waiting to Court. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hamakuchi, Takayuki** Crown Mem. of House of Peers, Vice-Min. of Finance in 2nd Okuma Cabinet, '14; b. '70 in Tosa; grad. Law, Imperial Tokyo Univ. '95; Director of the Tobacco Monopoly Bureau '07-'12; ex-Vice-Min. of Communications '12-'13. Add. Yarai-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hamao, Arata** Baron (cr. '07), Lord Steward to the Crown Prince Privy Councillor; ex-Pres. Imp. Univ. Tokyo, L.L.D. (Harvard), b. in Tajima, '49; Councillor of the Department of Education, '78; Vice-President of Tokyo University in '84; Director of the Special Education Bureau, '88; President of the Tokyo Imperial University, '93-'97; Minister of Education from Nov. to Dec. of 1897. Reappointed Pres. of the Univ. Dec. '05-'13 in consequence of the celebrated "Strike movement" of the Professors. Add. Kanatomi-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hanabusa, Naosaburo** D.C.L. (Japan), Dir. Statistic Bureau of the Cabinet, b. '57 in Okayama, brother of the below; appointed official of the Cabinet, then Secretary of the Privy Council, Personal Secretary to Marquis Ito, Secretary of the Cabinet, and finally appointed to the present post in '98. He travelled abroad to investigate statistic affairs, '02-'03, and is one of the authorities in this line. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hanabusa, Yoshikata** Viscount (Baron in '00 and Viscount in '07), ex-Vice-Minister of the Imperial Household Department, President of the Japan Red Cross Society, b. in Okayama, '42; Secretary for Foreign Affairs '70; chargé d'affaires at Seoul, '76-'77; promoted to Minister Resident '80-'81, and narrowly escaped from being killed by the anti-Japanese Korean mob that rose there. Minister to St. Petersburg, '83-'86; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, '87-'88, and then transferred to the Imperial Household as its chief auditor, and promoted to the other post, which he resigned in '09. Appointed to the present post in Jan. '13. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hanai, Takuzo** D.C.L. (Japan), Barrister, M.P. since '98, b. '68 in Bingo, grad. with honour from an English Law School in Tokyo when he was only eighteen years old; finished the University Course at the Hogakuin with honour in '87; passed Barristers' Examination a year or two after and soon attracted public notice for his scholarship and eloquence, especially in criminal affairs. Has written several popular essays on Criminal Law. Add. Nishiki-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hara, Rokuro** a prominent business-man, b. '44 in Tajima and played some distinguished part at the time of the Restoration, having fought on the Imperial side in the civil war; studied in England and U.S. '71-'77. Entered and founded with a few others the One Hundredth Bank and has been instrumental in pushing it to the present flourishing condition; filled the post of President of the Yokohama Specie Bank from '83 to '90; is acknowledged as a power in business circles, and is a director for a number of Companies. Add. Goten-yama, near Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n113/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Hara, Takashi** M.P., b. '54 at Morioka, entered the now defunct Law College, Justice Dep't., but left before graduation. He then took to journalism and was on the staff of the Hochi which he soon left to take charge of a newly started organ of the Clan interest; followed Marquis Inouye as newspapers correspondent when he was despatched to Korea '82 as Special Envoy, and was soon appointed afterward an official in the Foreign Office; Consul in Tientsin; Secretary and chargé d'affaires in Paris, '86; the Marquis' Personal Secretary when he was Minister of Agriculture and Commerce; next acted in same capacity when Count Mutsu filled the chair '90-'92; followed his chief to the Foreign Office to be made Director of the Commercial Bureau, and promoted to the Vice-Minister in '95 and transferred to Minister to Korea, '96. He resigned the post the following year and for somewhile took journalism as chief editor of the Osaka Mainichi. He was one of the right-hand men of Prince Ito when he raised the banner of the Seiyu-kai, '00, and filled as the late Hoshi's successor the chair of the Minister of Communications from Dec., '00 to May the following year. Then chief editor of the Osaka Shimpo, and appointed in Jan. '06 to the Ministerial post, which he resigned with the fall of the Seiyu-kai Cabinet '08. Visited Europe and America '08-'09, re-entered the Cabinet as Min. of Home Affairs '13-'14. Add. 4, Shiba Park; Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hara, Tomitaro** Pres. 2nd Bank, Head of the Hara Firm, Yokohama, b. in Shinano '70; studied at Waseda, Uni., was adopted into the present millionaire family as husband of its only daughter, is a Director of the Yokohama Fire Insurance Co. and is a leading young business-man; reputed for his fine collection of art objects. Add. Benten-dori, Yokohama. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hara, Yoshimichi** lawyer, Dr., D. C. L. (Japan) b. in Shinano, '67; graduated the Tokyo University in '89; Councillor, Dep't Agri. & Com. in '09; Chief of the Tokyo Mine Inspection Office and that of Osaka '01-03; has since been practising law. Add. Iida-machi, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Harada, Tasuku** Pres. Doshisha in Kyoto, since '07, b. '63, in Kumamoto; studied at the Foreign Language School in Kumamoto, and later at the Doshisha; entered Yale and grad. from Divinity School, class '91; the following year he travelled through Enrope; was Pastor in Tokyo Kyoto and Kobe; Editor of Rikugo Zasshi, and "The Christian World." Pres., of the Christian Endeavor Union of Japan '93-'06 and was present in the world convention in London, '00. Visited India, '06, by the invitation of the National Union of Y.M.C.A.; attended World Missionary Conference at Edinburgh and lectured at Harvard, Yale and other Universities in America '09. Received LL.D. from University of Edinburgh and D.D. from Amerhest College. Add. Doshisha, Kyoto. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Harada, Yutaka** ex-Commercial Agent in London, was b. in '70 in Hyogo-ken, grad. Law (Imp. Tokyo Univ.) in '96, then entered the service of the Mitsubishi and was its agent in London, Hongkong and Shanghai, and lately the chief of the Kobe Branch; occupied the London post in '10-'13. Add. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n114/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Haraguchi, Kaname** Dr. Eng. (Japan), R'ly Eng., ex-Adv. to the Viceroy of Hukwang Provinces, China; b. '51 in Nagasaki-ken; was one of the pioneer students sent abroad for study by the Government, having studied Engineering at Yale, '75-'79; served long in the Gov. R'ly service; Dir. of the Hakata Bay R'ly Co., '02; held the post of the Adviser to the Viceroy '06-'10. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Haraguchi, Kensai** Baron (cr. '07), Lieut.-Gen. (retired), Mem. House of Peers, b. '47 in Bungo; Sub-Lieut., '72; Maj.-Gen., '97 meanwhile having filled the post of Com. of 1st Reg. (Inf.,) Body Guards, Chief Staff to the 4th Division and Director of the Toyama Mil. School. Commanded the Saghalien Exp. Army in the late War. He was dispatched to Germany, '90 to investigate military affairs. Add. Sanaizaka-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Haruta, Gennojyo** Chief of the Tokyo Branch of the N.Y.K., 4th son of Shogoro Hori, a bannerman of the Tokugawa Shogun, and adopted by Mototaro Haruta; began his business career in Mitsubishi Co., as a clerk, to be promoted to the present post in '79, having once had charge of the Kobe and Osaka Branches. Add. Oban-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n115/mode/1up))
