Shinagawa Electric Light Co., again returned to the Mitsui '92, to be appointed Agent at Osaka; entered the present bank '96. He is a leader of the Doshi-kai. Add. Kitahama Bank, Osaka. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n134/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Iwatare, Kunihiko** Dir. of the Nippon Electric Co. (joint undertaking between Japanese and foreign capitalists in which the Western Electric Co., U.S., are prominent) was b. '54 in Fukuoka, and entered the present Co. on its formtion about '93. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Iwaya, Suyeo** author, novelist, and an Anderson of Japan, b. '70 in Tokyo, 3rd son of the late Iwaya Ichiroku, a noted Chinese scholar and caligraphist. Studied in Germany '98-'03, filling at the same time chair of Japanese in the Oriental Language School, Berlin. Has written and translated quite a large number of stories for juvenile readers, and is connected with the Hakubunkan, Publishers, and also the Waseda University. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Izawa, Shimpei** Dentist was b. at Shizuoka in '60; studied dentistry in U.S.A. and established a hospital of Kyobashi; has been appointed dentist attached to the Bureau of Court Physician. Add. Toriizaka-cho, Azabu, T. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Izawa, Shuji** Mem. House of Peers, was b. 1851 at Takato, Shinano; was in the Department of Education almost without a break '72-'97, as Secretary, Dir. of the Deaf, Dumb and School, Music School, Higher Normal School, etc., also Bureau Dir. Has rendered distinguished services for curing stammering, and was rewarded in 1911 with 3rd class Rising Sun for it. Add. Dairoku-ten, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Izawa, Takio** Metropolitan Chief Police Commissioner, was b. '69, in Nagano Pref.; graduated from Law College of Imp. Tokyo Univ. Metropolitan Police Inspector '06; Governor of Wakayama '07, of Ehime '08-'12, of Niigata '12-14; present post '14. Add. Metropolitan Police Board, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Jimbo, Kotaro** Geologist, Prof. at the Col. of Science of the Imp. Tokyo University, was b. '67 in Tokyo; grad. the above Col. in '89; Prof. at the 1st Higher School, etc., further studied in Germany; Prof. at the Tokyo University since '98, and has frequently been commissioned with geological survey in Korea, Saghalien, etc. Add. Komagome, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Jo, Kazuma** Pres. of Appeal Court, Seoul, was b. in 1864 in Tokyo, grad. Law, Imperial Tokyo University in '88, and at once began practice in which he has risen to the front. Has repeatedly been M.P. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kabayama, Aisuke** Dir. of the Hakodate Dockyard Company, son and heir of Admiral Count Kabayama, was born '65 in Kagoshima, studied in U.S.A.; and has lately been associated with some Anglo-Japanese business enterprises. Add. Nagata-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kabayama, Sukenori** Full Admiral, Count, (cr. Vis. '84 and Count '95), holder of the 2nd Golden Kite, Privy Councillor, b. in Satsuma, '37; entered the Army while young and on the occasion of the Civil War of '77 was on the Staff of Major-General Tani (then Commander of Kumamoto Army Division) as Lieut.-Col.; transferred to the Navy '84; promoted Vice-Admiral '85; appointed the Vice-Minister '87 and Minister of Naval Staff at the time of the Japan-China War, and on board the transport Saikyo-maru was present in the battle of the Yellow Sea of Sept. 16th, '94. He was the first Governor-General of Formosa: then appointed Minister of Home Affairs Sept. '96—Dec. '97; Minister of Education Nov. '93—Sept. 1900; Privy Councillor in '04, Chairman of the Education Investigation Committee in '13. Add. Nagata-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n135/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kadono, Chokuro** Mg. Dir., Okura Firm was b. '68 in Ise; and is brother of I. Kadono, grad. Civil Eng., Imp. Univ. Tokyo: has since been connected with the Firm and was its London Agent '00-'07, m. Miss Riyo, d. of Baron Megata; 2 S. Add. Shinsaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kadono, Rempachiro** Chief of the Trade Section of the Mitsui Bank; was b. in Miye-ken, in '68 entered the service of the bank in its early days and was promoted to the present post. Add. Kanasugihama-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kadono, [illegible]** Dir. of the Chiyoda Mutual Life Assurance Co., and of the Kyōdō Fire Ins. Co., etc.; was b. '56, in Miye-ken; was long connected with the Keio-Gijuku, being one of its pioneer graduates; took to business about '01, severing connection as instructor with the institution. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kadono, Tominosuke** Mg. Director of the Meiji Bank: was b. in Akita-ken in '97; studied in the former Tokyo Semmon Gakko, and grad. its Political and Economical Dep't., joined the staff of the Hochi Shimbun; was once Chairman of Nagoya Municipal Assembly; Vice-president of the Chamber of Commerce of the same City; visited America in '10 representing business men in Nagoya. Add. Meiji Ginko, Nagoya. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kagawa, Keizo** (cr. Viscount '87 and Count '07), b. '39 at Mito; became an official in the Department of Imp. Household in '70, to fill posts of Secretary, Dir. of the Peerage Bureau etc. and has been Lord Steward of the Empress' Household since '80. Add. Kici-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kaino, Kozo** M.P. and Director of South Man. Rly, was b. in '57; took active part in local politics before he was returned to the House where as a member of the Seiyukai he enjoys great influence. Add. Toyo-oka-cho, Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kaijima, Tasuke** a coal boss, b. '44 in Fukuoka-ken; owns Onoura and other collieries, and is a self-made man who has risen from a miner. Add. Naokata-cho, Fukuoka-ken. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kakami, Kenkichi** Manager of the Tokyo Marine Insurance Co., was b. in Tokyo in '68 and is brother of Koichiro Kakami, as below. Add. Otsuka, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kakami, Koichiro** Sectional Chief of the Nippon Yusen Kaisha, was b. '60 in Tokyo, and early entered the service of the Company. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kakchi, Mototada** Inspector of the Japan Beer Brewery Co.; was born in '43 at Takasu: was for many years judge or procurator at the Appeal Courts and the Court of Cassation; retired from the bench in '98 and has entered business. Add. Araki-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kakeshita, Fujiro** Judge of the Supreme Court; was b. in Saga-ken in '57; Judge in '85; Judge of the Tokyo Appeal Court '94, the sectional chief of the Osaka Appeal Court; transferred to the present position in '98. Add. Nishi-Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kakigami, Junshiro** Auditor of the Yokohama Fire Marine, Trans portation, Trust. Ins. Co.; was b. in Gunma-ken in '47; engaged in business since early age and is a prominent businessman in Yokohama. Add. Minami-Otamachi, Yokohama. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n136/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kakinuma, Tanizo** Dir., Tokyo Gassed Yarn Mill, etc.; was b. in Joshu, '54 of a well-to-do fish merchant, is one of the few millionaire yarn merchants in the city. Add. Ko-amicho, Honjo, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kakizaki, Tomisaburo** Major-General, Commander of the 2nd Brigade, Infantry; was b. in Fukushima-ken; Sub-Lieut. in '83; Capt. in '93; Lieut.-Col. in '03; Maj.-Gen. in '09; holder of the order of the Sacred Treasure and of the Rising Sun. Add. Utsunomiya Brigade. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kako, Kakusho** Physician, was b. in Chiba-ken in '55; grad. medical Col. of the Imp. University, Tokyo, in '81; has since been engaged in the line of ear-diseases. Add. Ogawa-machi, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kamada, Katsutaro** Member of the House of Peers as highest tax payer; was b. in Kagawa in '62; occupied posts of director or Chairman of various Cos. and assembly in his locality. Add. Kami-niban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kamada, Yeikichi** Mem. House of Peers, and of the Highest Edu. Council, b. '58 in Kii, from which he was returned once to the House of Representatives, March '94; studied at the Keiogijuku '74-'76; Principal of Kagoshima Zoshikan High School '81-'83; taught for the third time at the Keiogijuku, to be given a post at the Home Office '86, which however he soon resigned, and was appointed Director of Oita-Middle School, but returning once more to his alma mater '89, by which he was sent abrcad to inspect educational affairs in Europe and America, to be appointed its head on returning home, is reputed as one of the best after-dinner speakers in Japan. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kamei, Ijo** Count and descendant of the house of the daimyo who formerly held the fief of Tsuwano; b. '84 in Tokyo. Add. Maruyama, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kameoka, Taishin** Maj.-General (ret.) was b. in '52 at Utsunomiya, Sub-Lieutenant in '78; entered the Staff College in '83, Lieut.-Colonel in '01, Colonel in '03, Commander of 50th Regiment in the late War, Major-General in '08 and placed on retired service. Add. 6-chome, Minami-cho, Ak., T. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kamiki, Jisaburo** Director of the Kamiki Branch established by this family, was b. in '70 in Tokyo. Add. Hashiba, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kamimura, Hikonojo** Admiral, Mil. Supreme Councillor, Baron ('07) 1st class Golden Kite; b. in Satsuma; entered the Imp. Navy while young and was the Captain of the cruiser Akitsushima in the Japan-China War of '94-'95; Captain of the battleship Asahi '99; promoted Vice-Admiral in '03; was Com.-in-Chief of the 2nd Japanese Squadron in the late war and sank the Rurik and seriously damaged the Gromboi and the Rossia in the engagement fought off the eastern coast of Korea on Aug. 14th '05. Removed to Commander of Yokosuka Admiralty in Dec. '05; Adm. '10, to the Commander of 1st Squadron in '08 and in '11 to the present post. Add. Kami-Meguro, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kamimura, Keikichi** Rear-Adm., Commander of Ōminato Naval Station; was b. 1869; midshipman in '87. Add. Ōminato, Aomori-ken. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n137/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kamio, Koshin** Lieut.-Gen. and Com. of 18th Division in Kurume, was b. '55 in Nagano-ken; entered the then existing Non-Com. Officers' School '74; Sub-Lieut. '79; held post of Chief Staff to 1st Army and 10th Army Div., besides being attached for a long while to the Japanese Legation at Peking; was staff officer to the 2nd Army in the Japan-China War and of Marshal Oyama in the late war commanded for a while the Japanese Garrison in China. Add. Kurume. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kamiya, Denbei** President of the Japan Transport Co., Auditor of the Tokyo Rly; and well-known as wine-brewer, was b. in '55 in Ibaragi; started a provision store and made fortune by selling a cheap sweet wine; possesses a large vineyard near Mito. Add. Hanakawa-do, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kamiyama, Mitsunoshin** Vice-Min. of Agr. and Commerce, was born in Yamaguchi in 1870, graduated Law at Imperial Tokyo University class '95, was Secretary and Judge at the Administrative Litigation Court; Director of Forestry Bureau. Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce before his transfer to the Gov. of Kumamoto ('12-13) app. to present post April '14. Add. Saka-machi, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kanai, En** Doctor of Law (Japan); was b. in Shizuoka-ken, in '65; graduated from Col. of Literature of the Tokyo Imp. University in '85; the next year he went to England and Germany to study law and economics; professor at his alma mater '90; dispatched to Europe '11. Add. Tennoji-machi, Yanaka, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kanda, Naibu** Baron, b. Tokyo in '57, adopted son of the late Kohei Kanda, a pioneer Dutch scholar and Gov. official who was created Baron shortly before his death. The future Baron proceeded in '71 to U.S.A. for study and returned home about '84 after having grad. Amherst Col., Mass., was appointed Prof. in the Tokyo Imp. Univ. and 1st High School, but now Prof. at the Higher Com. School; is a Mem. House of Peers. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kaneko, Kentaro** Viscount (Baron '95, Viscount '07) 1st class Rising Sun, Privy Councillor, hon. LL.D. (Harvard) President of the America's Friends Society, was b. at Fukuoka '53; grad. Harvard University, U.S.A. class '78: Private Secretary to Ito when he was Premier '85-'86, next when he was President of the Privy Council '88-'90; Chief Secretary of the House of Peers '90; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce '94-'96; and finally Minister of Agriculture and Commerce Apr.-June '98 and Minister of Justice Oct, 1900—May '01. On the outbreak of the late war he was sent to U.S.A. to represent Japan non-officially, and returned home soon after the conclusion of peace. He was chairman of the Japan Grand Exhibition to be held in '12, but with its postponement to '17 by the Katsura Cabinet, he resigned the post. Add. Ichibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kanno, Katsunosuke** Dir. of the Gov. Printing Bureau; was b. in '67 in Gifu-ken; grad. law at the Imperial Tokyo Univ., in '96; clerk in the Finance Dep't in that year and passed the higher civil service examination the following year; secretary of the Dep't. of Agr. and Com., to be transferred soon after to a similar post in the Treasury; attached to the Embassy in London in '07 and next transferred to the present post. Add. Saka-machi, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kano, Hisanobu** Viscount, mem. House of Peers, b. '48, brother of Viscount Tachibana and adopted into the present family which hereditarily held the fief of Ichinomiya Kazusa. Began his career as official of the Edu. Dep't and Dir. of Normal School; next entered juridical service and held a post of Judge and then that of Public Procurator at various local courts and ultimately at an Appeal Court; finally transferred to post of Gov. of Kagoshima which he occupied a good number of years. Since his retirement from active work, he has been associated with various enterprises intended to promote industry. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n138/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kano, Jigoro** President of Tokyo Higher Normal School, was b. at Higo, '60; Director and Professor of the Peers' School '85; Director of the Special Education Bureau '98; President of Tokyo Higher Normal School since '01. He is a renowned master of the art of Jujitsu and most of the noted young Jujitsu experts have studied at the Jujitsu Institute founded by the subject of the sketch. Add. Tokyo Higher Normal School. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kano, Kyokichi** D. Litt. (Japan), Dean of Col. Literature, Imp. Univ., Kyoto; was b. 1866 in Tokyo, grad. Col. Sc. '84 and next Col. Lit. '89; Prof. of 4th High School, etc., Pres. 1st High School, transferred to the present post '07. Add. Imp. Kyoto Univ. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kano, Tomonosuke** Man. Dir. of the Tokyo Branch of the Sumitomo Bank, b. '72 in Ibaraki-ken; was for a while Secretary of the House of Representatives and Councillor to the Dep't of Ag. and Com.; entered the Sumitomo Bank '00. Add. Sumitomo Bank, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kano, Yunoshin** Baron (cr. '07) Vice-Adm. and ex-Commander of Mekong Naval Station, Formosa, was b. '54 at Matsuyoda, Shinshu; midshipman in '77; Captain in '97; Rear-Adm. in '03 and Vice-Adm. in '08; was a commissioner charged to bring home the Matsushima from France '91 commanded the Saikyo Maru in the Battle of Yellow Sea in '94. Add. Naval Office, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Katakura, Kentaro** greatest silk manufacturer in Suwa district, Shinshu, and in the whole of Japan head of the Katakura Firm, b. '52 at his paternal seat in Kawakishi, Suwa; succeeded to the family estate '80 and by his untiring diligence and able management has attained prosperity; owns 13 filatures, 17 branch offices and gives employment to thousands of men and women, producing over 5,000 bales of raw silk per annum. Add. Hommura-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kataoka, Shichiro** Baron, Mil. Sup. Councillor, Adm. b. '53, 2nd son of retainer of the Lord of Satsuma; Midshipman '77; held posts of Commander of various cruiser, chief Staff Officer to a Standing Squadron etc. Commanded the 3rd Squadron in the late war: Chief of Naval Construction Board in '06; Commander of Maizuru, Adm. '10. Add. Onden, near Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Katayama, Kuniyoshi** D.M., b. '55 in Shizuoka-ken; studied medical jurisprudence in Austria and Germany '84-'88, and has been Prof. at the Col. of Med. of Im. Tokyo Univ. since that time. He is at present vice-chairman of the Dojin-kai, a medical association for befriending China and Korea. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Katayama, Toguma** Chief of Architectural Bureau, Imperial Household, b. '53 in Yamaguchi-ken; finished in '79 Architecture at the the now defunct Engineering College; commissioned to build Prince Arisngawa's residence, '81; built the Japanese Legation at Peking '86. Has undertaken the construction of the Crown Prince's Palace completed after about ten years' work. He has frequently gone abroad to study his speciality. Add. Oulen, near Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n139/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kato, Hiroyuki** Baron, (cr. 1900). D. Litt. and D.C.L. (Japan), Privy Councillor, Mem. House of Peers since '90, b. in Tajima, '36; is one of the pioneer scholars of Occidental learning and by translation of original work has done much in introducing Occidental civilization into this country; has written various works on evolution and against Christianity. For many years down to '93, he was President of the Imperial University at Tokyo and is the first privy Councillor who has risen to this honourable position for erudition. Add. Kami-Nibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kato, Masuo** b. '54 in Miye-ken; was a clerk of the Foreign Office '77 and removed to be Chancellor of the Legation at Rome '84, next to the Legation at the Hague; promoted Councillor at the Foreign Office '91; was appointed Consul at Fusan '04, then removed to Seoul first as Secretary of the Legation and afterward its chief. Was for a long while adviser of the Korean Government; has lately organized a company to exploit Korea. Add. Nakano-cho, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kato, Masayoshi** Vice-President of the Nippon Yusen Kwaisha (Japan Mail Steamship Company), b. in '54; was for some while a clerk at Local Offices; entered '85 the newly formed (by amalgamation of the Mitsubishi and the Kyodo Unyu) Nippon Yusen Kwaisha with the late Mr. Morioka, its first President; was elected its Director '89, and Managing Director '93, and then the present post. Was Pres. of the ex-Hunan S.S. Co. organized by Japanese capitalists, and travelled abroad '06-'07 to inspect shipping business. Add. Motozono-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kato, Motoshiro** ex-Consul-General at Mukden till 1909, b. '70 in Bungo, passed Consular and Diplomatic Service Examination in '95, attaché at Seoul in '95, élève Consul at London in '96, Consul at Suchow, China, in '99, transferred to Hongkong in '00, to Ninsen '01, Consul-General at Tientsin in '06. Add. Foreign Office. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kato, Sadakichi** Vice-Admiral, Chief of the Naval Arsenal, Yokosuka; was b. '61 in Tokyo-fu; naval Sub-Lieut. 2nd class in '86; Captain '02 and Rear-Adm. '07; was at one time Staff Officer of the Standing Squadron, Adjutant to Admiral of the Fleet, Vice-Com. of the Akitsushima, Kasagi and Yakumo etc. Add. Yokosuka. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kato, Taka-akira** ex-Minister of Foreign Affairs, Baron (cr. '11), was b. 1859 at Nagoya, and grad. Law, the Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '81. After a year or two in the Mitsubishi, he entered the Foreign Office '87 and appointed personal secretary to the Foreign Minister (then Count Okuma) '88; on the resignation of his chief, he was transferred to the Finance Office to be promoted in time to be the Director of the Banking Bureau, that of the Taxation Bureau, etc. Soon he returned to the Foreign Office and from '94 to '99 he represented Japan in England; Minister of Foreign Affairs in 1900-'01; sat twice in the House, once as M.P. for Kochi-ken elected (Aug. '02) unsolicited and next in March '03 from Yokohama. Entered the Saionji Cabinet as For. Minister, but to resign in Mar., being opposed to the Railway Nationalization. Ambassador at London and then For. Min., Dec. '12-Feb. '13. Was decorated with G.C.M.G. by the late King Edward. He leads the Doshikai formed by late Prince Katsura. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Kojimachi-ku, T. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n140/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kato, Terumaro** physician-in-ordinary to Court, eldest son of Baron H. Kato, was b. '63, studied medicine at Tokyo Imp. Univ., and further at Berlin and Vienna, his specialty being children's diseases. Add. Hommura, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kato, Tomosaburo** Vice-Adm., Com'der of Kure Adm'ity, b. 59 in Hiroshima; midshipman '83; Captain '99; Prof. at the Naval Academy; Construction-Superviser and Sectional Chief at the Navy before he was appointed '02 to be Chief of Staff office of a Standing Squadron; chief of staff of the Kamimura Squadron in the late war; next transferred to a Bureau Chief, appointed Vice-Minister in '06; Vice-Adm. in '08; to present office '09. Add. Kure. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kato, Tsunetada** M.P. ex-Minister at Brussel, b. '59 at Matsuyama; was attache at Paris, 86; secretary, For. Officer '91, and was again transferred as Secretary to the Legation at Paris, '92-'97. Recalled home to be appointed Chief of Personal Affairs, promoted to be Minister Resident 3 year later, and to full Minister-Plenitentiary accredited to Belgium, '02. Resigned the post in '07 and became editor of the Osaka Shimpo. Holds 3rd Sacred Treasure. Add. Osaka Shimpo, Osaka. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Katsura, Jiro** Dir. of varicus Cos., was b. 1856 in Yamaguchi, brother of Prince Katsura; studied wine brewing in Germany and France and started brewing business at Sapporo and in Yamanashi, but it has failed; has subsequently turned a business-man. Add. Sakuragawa. Nishikubo, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kawada, Takashi** Mg. Dir. of Keio Electric R'ly Co., &c., eldest son of Takeshi Kawada, learned scholar of Chinese classics, b. '70 and studied at Michigan in '96. Add. Wakamiya-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kawai, Misao** Maj. Gen., Dir. of Personnel Bureau, War Office; was b. 1864; Sub.-Lieut. of Infantry 1886; grad. from the Staff College 1897; Maj.-Gen. 1900. Add. Naitomachi, Y., T. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kawai, Shintaro** Dendrologist, Prof. at the Ag. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo University; b. '65 in Owari; grad. Ag. Col. in '92; Assis.-Prof. at the College '94; was honored with the doctorate in '99; sent to Germany and Austria to prosecute his study for about three years, returning home '03 and made full Prof. at the same College. Add. Meguro, near Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kawakami, Kin-ichi** Gen.-Man. Sumitomo Firm, b. Kumamoto '37; grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '78; entered the Foreign Office, rising to be Consul-Gen. at Shanghai, then Dir. Commercial Bureau; entered the Nippon Ginko as its Bureau Dir. which he resigned '98 to enter the Sumitomo's service, Osaka. Add. Sumitomo, Osaka. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kawakami, Toshihiko** ex-Consul-Gen. at Harbin '07-14, and Mng. of the South Manchuria Rly since 1914, was b. '61 in Tokyo, clerk at Fusan, Vladivostock, San Francisco and St. Petersburg in '86-'92; Interpreter at last place in '96; Commercial Agent at Vladivostock in '00, Legation Sec. in '04, attached to Commanding Headquarters in Liaotung in '04; Commercial Agent in Vladivostock in '06, was injured by stray shot when late Prince Ito was assassinated in '09. Edu. gra'ted Russia from the Tokyo Foreign Language School in '84. Add. Dairen, South Manchuria Rly. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Hoshu** Surgeon-Major-General of the Navy, was b. '59 in Usuki, Oita-ken, second-assistant-surgeon in '74; took part in the battle of 77 ; wounded on board the Matsushima in the China-Japan War when he was Chief Surgeon of the Standing Squadron; Chief of the Naval Medical Board at Sasebo, Yokosuka, and then at Kure; placed on reserve list in '06. Add. Shiroyama-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n141/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kawamura, Jozaburo** ex-Vice-Min. of Justice, b. '59 in Shigaken; grad. the then existing Law Institute of Justice Dep't '84; sent abroad for further study '86-'90; appointed Councillor of Justice; Judge at some District Courts, at Tokyo App. Court, a Public Pro. at Supreme Court, Dir. of the Civ. and Cri. Bureau, appointed Vice-Min. of Justice in Jan. '06 and held the post till '11. Add. Minami-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Kageaki** General, Visc., (cr. '07), b. '59 in Satsuma; commanded a Brigade in the Japan-China War; and was afterward appointed commander of the First Army Division (Tokyo); he led the Himeji Division in the late War, and was then appointed the commander of an Army which formed the extreme right in the Battle of Mukden. Appointed Mem. Supreme Military Council Dec. '05. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Kingoro** Vice-Min. of Imp. Household and ex-Chief Sec. of Privy Council, was b. in '63 in Mimasaku, grad. with honor from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '87; secretary at local and central offices and Privy Council till about '08, Chief Sec. in '08 and Vice-Min. in '10. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Sozaburo** Lieut.-Gen., Commander of 9th Division, was b. in 1858 at Kagoshima; Sub.-Lieut. of Infantry 1889; present appointment 1912, having in the meanwhile commanded the 16th and the 9th Brigade. Add. Kanagawa. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki, Hachiroemon** Pres. of Kawasaki Bank and other concerns; b. in Tokyo '66; grad. Tokyo Higher Commercial School and further studied in America. Assumed his father's name Hachiro-emon in '13. Add. Kawasaki Bank, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki, Hajime** President of the Nippon Fire Ins. Co. since 1913 and Dir. of the Kawasaki Bank, was b. 1884 in Tokyo; entered the Tokyo Higher Commercial School in 1893, and next the Pensylvania University for Economics, class 1909. Add. Sangenya, Azabu. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawashima, Chunosuke** a Director of the Yokohama Specie Bank, b. '55 in Tokyo; studied French and naval architecture at the Gov. Dockyard, Yokosuka; was engaged as interp. and clerk '71 by a Dutch firm, Yokohama; appointed agent at Lyons by the Yokohama Specie Bank '79-'93; removed to the Head Office '94; Agent at Bombay till '95; Agent in Tokyo, '02-'03; than recalled to the Head Office. Add. Fukuyama-cho, Hongo, T. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawashima, Reijiro** Vice-Adm., Director of the Hydrographic Dept., was b. '64 in Ishikawa-ken; Sub-Lieut. 2nd class '88; Captain in '01 and Rear-Adm. in '07. Com. of 3rd Squadron, '11; sent to China as Com. of our detachment during the Chinese revolution '11 and then to the present post in 1912. Was decorated with 3rd class of Golden Kite for his service in the late War. Add. Takanawa, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kawata, Ryukichi** Baron, Pres. of the Yokohama Dockyard, b. '56, eldest son of the late Baron Kawata, Gov. of the Nippon Ginko and founder of the house; he succeeded to the title on the death of his father in '96. The present Baron having a great interest in mechanical science chose shipbuilding as his profession. Add. Shin Ogawa-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n142/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kazan-in, Chikaiye** Marquis, of a former courtier family which was founded in the 12th century. The present head was b. in 1878, 7th son of Vis. Horikawa and adopted into the family which had no male issue. Add. Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kido, Takamsa** ex-Chief Chamberlain to the Crown Prince, Marquis, b. 1856, adopted son of Kido Koin (1824-'77), one of the great personages in the work of Restoration and created Peer for the service. The present head studied mining in U.S.A. 1870-74, afterward at the Tokyo Univ. and also Osaka Techinical Institute. Add. Shinzaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kikkawa, Chokichi** Baron, mem. House of Peers since 1893, is a branch of the House of Vis. Kikkawa, and was b. '59, 3rd son of the former chief of the said house and uncle to the present Viscount; granted Barony and orderd to establish separate house in recognition of his father's service at the time of the Restoration; studied at Harvard, U.S.A. and returned home '83 to hold posts of attache, Councillor and Sec. at the Foreign Office. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kikoshi, Yasutsuna** Baron (cr. '07), 2nd Order of Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor). Lieut.-Gen., ex-Min. of War (Dec. '12-Jun. '13); b. at Kanazawa in '54; he entered the Military Academy '75; Ensign '77; sent abroad for study in Germany '82; Major-Gen. '98 and Com. of 23rd Brigade '01. Took part in the Japan-China War, which procured him 4th Order Golden Kite, and also in the expedition occasioned by the Boxer trouble; fought under Gen. Oku at the head of his brigade in the late war and was raised to succeed his Chief on the latter's removal to a higher post. Attended Jamestown Mil. and Nav. Exhibition. Add. Kagacho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Choshiro** Pres, Tokai Bank, b. '53; prop. of big dry-goods store in the business quarter of Tokyo; is a Director of various companies, once sat in the House of Peers as highest tax payer representative for Tokyo Prefecture. Add. Takecho, Shita, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Dairoku** M.A. (Cambridge), Baron (cr. '02), D. Sc. (Japan), Privy Councillor (1912) ex-President of the Imperial Kyoto University ('08-'12), Pres. of Teikoku Gakushi-in (Imperial Academy), b. '55 in Mimasaka; was one of those sent '66 by the Tokugawa Gov. to England for study, to return home with its downfall, but again sent '70 by the Imp. Gov. Graduated mathematics at Cambridge, '77 as one of the wranglers; was next Prof. of mathematics, then President of the Imp. Univ. and lately Minister of Education June 1901-July '03; President of the Peers' School from '04 to '05. Delivered lectures on Japanese education at the University of London, 1909 and at Civic Forum, U.S.A., '10. Add. Motomachi, H. T. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Jozaburo** Surg.-Gen. ret. b. '55 in Saga, joined the rank of Army Surgeons in '80, and ultimately raised to General's status attached to the Osaka Division; placed on retired list in '05. Add. Tanimachi, Higashi, Osaka. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Takenori** M.P., b. in Aomori '67, edu. at Keio Gijuku, was journalist connected with the Jiji '90-'95, General Affairs Chief of the Kyushu R'ly '95-'02, M.P. for Moji '03, and then for Aomori. Add, Minami Odawara-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n143/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kikuchi, Tsunesaburo** Surg.-Gen. (ret.) M.D., Dir. of Kwaisei Hospital at Nishinomiya, near Osaka; b. '55 in Saga-ken; grad. at Medicine, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '81, and entered Army as surgeon, and attained the highest post of Surg. Lieut.-Gen. in '07. Took part in the Japan-China and Russo-Japanese wars as Chief Surg. Add. Nishinomiya, Osaka. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kikutei, Kōchō** Marquis, head of the former house of a courtier. Add Bancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kimata, Tani** Major.-General, Prof. at the Military Gunnery and Engineering School, was b. '57 in Okayama-ken; appointed Sub.-Lieut. of Engineer in '77; sent to France '86 to study military science for several years; was also instructor in the Military Cadets' School; Staff-Officer of the 4th Division, etc. Add. Tani-machi, Ichigaya, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kimbara, Meizen** a millionaire and public benefactor, b. in Enshu '32; has undertaken several works of public utility, for which he has repeatedly been rewarded by the Government. Early in '08 he retired to his native village and became its headman; owns a bank, stationery and toilet oil shops. Add. Kimbara Bank, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kimotsuki, Keako** Baron, (cr. '07) Vice-Adm. (ret.), Mg. Imp. Lifeboat Society, Lecturer at Naval Col., was b. 1853 in Kagoshima; Sub-Lieut. '72; held posts of Hydrographical Section, Pres. of Naval Col. etc. Was Mayor of Osaka, '12-'13. Add. Shimo Nibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Riemon** b. '34, is one of the millionaires of Yokohama, who, by foresight and shrewdness, rose from obscurity to the present opulence by selling raw silk to foreigners early in Meiji era; is connected with the Yokohama Electric Light Co., the Specie Bank and other important establishments; once sat in the House of Peers as high-tax paying member for Kanagawa-ken. Add. Benten-dori, Yokohama. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Sakaye** D. Sc. (Japan), M.R.A.S. (Eng.), Dir. of the Gov. Geodetic Observatory at Mizusawa, since, '00, was b. '68 in Kanazawa; grad. Astronomy, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '92; attended the World's Geodetic Conference '98, also next conference held in '06, when he read an essay pointing out error in the formula relating to the axis, and his discovery has been acknowledged as Kimura term by the world's astronomers; received first prize by the Imperial Academy, Japan, in '11. Add. Mizusawa, Japan. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Seishiro** Departmental Dir. of the Bank of Japan, was born '51 in Okayama Prefecture. Had once been an editor of the "Chugai Shogyo," next he entered the present bank in '03, where he began to distinguish himself and was appointed its director soon afterward. Add. Minami Sakuma-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Shunkichi** Naval Eng. and expert of wireless telegraphy; b. '66; grad. course of physics at the Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '89; was instructor at Tokyo and Sendai High Schools 1893-6, studied at Harvard under late Prof. J. M. Peirce for one year and at Yale under late Prof. J. Gibbs, specially in the line of quaternions and its physical applications; founder with Prof. J. Molenbrack in Holland, of the International Association for promoting the study of quaternions and allied subjects, which continues to this time. Prof. at the Naval Academy 1900 and member of the Wireless Telegraph Committee; dispatched to America and Europe '01 to investigate the wireless telegraph service; had charge of naval wireless telegraph laboratory near Yokosuka Jan. '03, when he hit upon a process of wirless telegraphy and completed it early 1906, his system having been used exclusively during the War, by the Imperial Navy. He attended the International Wireless Telegraphy Conference held in fall '06 at Berlin; was decorated with Middle Cordon Rising Sun (annuity ¥360) for his service in the late War. Add. Yokosuka. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n144/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kimura, Sosuke** Naval Surgeon-General and Chief of the Medical Bureau, Navy; was b. '54 in Kagoshima, eldest son of Kyutaro, retainer of the clan. Add. Kasumi-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Yuko** Lieut.-Gen, Com. of 18th Division, was b. in '51 in Tokushima, Sub.-Lieut. in '72, Major-Gen. in '01, commanded 16th Brigade in the late War, Brigade Commander of the Bodyguards in '07, Lieut.-Gen. '09. Add. Fukuoka, Kyushu. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kinkozan, Sobei** celebrated potter of Awata, Kyoto, and 7th of the line, b. '68, 2nd son of Kinkozan VI by whom the export of Awata ware was started. The business at one time suffered decline, but by indefatigable effort and untiring perseverance the present head has restored and further carried the business to great prosperity, his exhibits having won more than once high-grade medals at various exhibitions both home and abroad. Add. Awata, Kyoto. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kinoshita, Tosaku** M.D. (Japan), Prof., Osaka Medical Coll., b. 1878 in Kyoto; Grad. from Imp. Univ. of Tokyo in 1899; studied physiology in Austria for a few years, returned home '10 and was granted the degree by presenting a thesis to his alma mater. Add. Osaka Med. Col., Osaka. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kinoshita, Yoshio** Traffic Manager, Imp. Govt. Rlys., b. 1874 in Tamba, Grad. Civil Engineering Course in Tokyo Imp. Univ., '98 entered Traffic Dept., Imp. Govt. Rlys.; studied practical and theoretical side of railway traffic in Europe and America 1905-'7. Add. Imp. Govt. Rly., Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kinoshita, Yuzaburo** Judge of Adm. Litigation Court, was b. in Wakayama in '64, grad. Law Course, Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '89, entered civil service and was a Governor in Formosa about '96. Add. Okubo, near T. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kishi, Seiichi** D.C.L. (Japan), Barrister, Dir. of the Osaka Gas Co., legal adviser to several Gov. and private institutions; was born '67 in Matsuye; grad. Law at Imp. University of Tokyo in '87 and began practice in Tokyo and soon grew to be one of the foremost lawyers. Add. Isarago, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kishigami, Kenkichi** Zoologist, Head of the Investigation Section, Fishery Bureau, Dep't of Agr. and Com., was born '67 in Aichi-ken; grad. Science Col. of Imp. University of Tokyo in '94; appointed technical expert of the said Dep't '95; sent to Europe and America in '97. Add. Ichiban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kitagaki, Kunimichi** Baron, Mem. House of Peers, ex-Pres. of Hokkaido R'ly. Co., b. '36 in Tottori; began his civil career in '69; afterward Secretary of the Home Department, then Governor of several prefectures, last appointed Vice-Minister of Home Affairs which he resigned in '99. Add. Kyoto. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n145/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kitagawa, Reisuke** Man. Dir. of Chiyoda Mutual Life Ins. Co., Dir. of the Tamagawa Electric R'ly Co., was b. '61 in Fukui-ken; was once editor of the Jiji. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Kitakoga, Takeichiro** Rear-Adm., ex-Chief, Torpedo Corps, Yokosuka; was b. '58 in Saga-ken; Ensign '83, promoted to the present rank, meanwhile filling the posts of Superintendent of Naval Ordinance, Sectional Chief of the Tokyo Naval Arsenal; Chief of the Kure Arsenal, etc. Was granted 4th class of Golden Kite '06; placed on retired service '09. Add. Osakimura, near Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Kitamura, Shigemasa** Prop. of the Seiyoken Hotels (at Ueno Park and Tsukiji) was b. 1872; grad. from the Meiji Gakuin in '93 and further studied at a private law school. The Seiyoken at Tsukiji, the first foreign restaurants in Tokyo, was established in '71 by his grandfather with the support of the late Prince Sanjo and some others. The Ueno Seiyoken was established in '73. Add. Unemecho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Kitazato, Shibasaburo** M.D., President of the Japan Medical Society, b. at Kumamoto '59; studied in Germany under Prof. Koch '85-'91; discovered the germs of tetanus, diphtheria, pest (in conjunction with Dr. Aoyama), etc., and is regarded as one of the foremost bacteriologists of the world; has charge of the Gov. blood-serum Laboratory, was bestowed with the Red Eagle by the Kaiser in '09 in appreciation of his distinguished scientific achievement. Add. Nakano-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Kiuchi, Jushiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers ('11), ex-Director of Agr. and Ind., Korea, b. '65 in Kazusa; graduated Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '88, and further studied at the Univ. Hall. Subsequently held posts at Legislative Bureau, House of Peers and finally appointed Dir. Commercial Bureau, Dep't. Agri. & Com., but owing to the opposition of businessmen against reorganizing the Stock Exchanges along the line of Bourse carried cut by him, he had to resign in '03; was a Bureau Dir., Korean Residency-Gen. from '06-'11, when he resigned. Add. Yobancho, Kojimachi, T. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Kiyosu, Iyenori** Count, ex-Governor of Niigata Prefecture, Mem. of the House of Peers, Dir. of the Japan Red Cross Society, is 12th son of the late Prince Kuniiye Fushimi, and was born '62 in Kyoto. In '88, he was ordered to create the Kiyosu family and was appointed Lord Abbot of Bukkoji in Kyoto '73, but afterward he turned a layman; Mem. of the House of Peers '90; Governor of Yamanashi '97, that of Ibaraki '98, and afterward to the same of Niigata-ken. Add. Shimo-6-bancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Kiyoura, Keigo** Visc., Privy Council, was b. '50 at Kumamoto; was at one time a schoolmaster; then a humble clerk in the central Gov. where his promotion was quite rapid, chiefly in the Justice and Home Offices. In the former he rose to the highest post and twice held the chair of Ministry '98-1900 and '01-'03; was transferred to that of Agr. and Com. in July '03 which he resigned '06. He is one of the trusted lieutenants of Marshal Prince Yamagata; created Baron in '02 in connection with the conclusion of the Anglo-Japanese treaty and appointed to the present post in '07. Add. Iriari Omori, nr. Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Koba, Sadanaga** D.C.L. (Japan), Crown Member of the House of Peers, b. in '59; studied in Germany and Austria, '82-'85; Private Secretary to the Minister of Education, &c., '85-'92; afterwards the Director of the Ordinary Education Bureau and Chief Councillor Education; was made Vice-Minister of education '08, which he resigned '05. Add. Harajuku, Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n146/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kobara, Tsutaye** Lt.-Gen., Chief of Artillery and Engineering School; was b. in Yehime-ken; graduated from Military Cadets School, 83, then from Military Staff College; studied in Germany till '95; Attache to German Legation, Instructor in Military College, Staff officer to 12th Division, Comm. of Tsushima garrison, before to present post. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Becker** lawyer, legal adviser, Director of some companies, is an English-born naturalized Japanese; was b. in 1863 and came to Yokohama in '87; became naturalized, '92; began law practice '94; has written several works elucidating Japanese laws. Add. Yamashita-cho, Yokohama. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Genzo** M.P., ex-Dir. of the Imperial Railways, was b. in 1867 in Yonezawa, and adopted in 1894 by Y. Kobayashi of the same place; grad. from Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo University in '96, appointed a Secretary of the Imperial Railways the following year and continued in the service till '12. He was one of those civilians who were on board the transport Hitachi Maru and taken prisoner by the Rosia in the late war. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Koai** member of the Metropolitan Council of Tokyo, was b. in '45 in Tokyo, and served for a long while at the Treasury in which he rose to the position of a Bureau Director, which he resigned 1891. He has been on the Board of Directors of various companies. Add. Yushima, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Usaburo** D.C.L. (Japan) an economist and financier, Dir. of the Japan Steel Foundry, Prof. at the Meiji Univ., etc. was b. 1865 in Gumma; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., 1894; Secretary at the Legislative Bureau '96-'07; Chief of the Financial Bureau, Formosa, 07-'10. Has written a number of works on finance and economy. Add. Akebono-cho, Hg., T. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Koda** a remarkable family of artists. "ROBAN," b. '67 is one of master novelists, a George Meredith of Japan. Among his famous works are "Gojunoto," "Futsuka Monogatari," etc. His elder brother, LIEUT. GUNJI (ret.) undertook the colonization of northerly islands of Kuriles, in 1894, and was taken prisoner at Kamchatka during the late war, but released in '05; his younger brother, NARIMOTO, is a grad. of the Col. Lit. Imp. Tokyo Univ., two sisters Miss NOBU, and Mrs. Ando (her younger sister) were b. at Tokyo, the elder in '70 and the younger '75; both showed a great talent for music for childfood; sent abroad by the Gov. to study music, Miss Nobu, to study in U.S.A., Germany and Austria '89-'95; then Profs. of the Gov. Tokyo Music Academy. Both have resigned. "Roban" was Lec. Col. Lit., Imp. Kyoto Univ., and resigned '08-'10; D. Litt. (Japan). Add. Mukojima, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kodama, Hideo** Count, Sectional chief of the Residency-General, Korea, was b. in '76 in Tokyo, eldest son of the late celebrated General Kodama, chief of staff in the late War; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo University in 1900 and has since been in the service at above. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n147/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kodera, Kenkichi** Lieut., (cav., ret), D.C.L., M.P., was b. '77 in Kobe; edu. Kobe Com. School, Colombia, Johns Hopkin's, and further in Germany etc., went through one-year volunteer service and was sent to the front in the late war. Add. Aoyama Minamicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Kodera, Shinsaku** Secretary at the Foreign Office, was b. in '55 at Himeji, grad. Literature, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '80, studied in England and Germany '81-'86, Prof. at the Tokyo Higher Normal School till '91 when he removed to the Foreign Office, was at the Legation, London '94-'95, then in St. Petersburg, Peking etc. Add. Sugamo-Myogizaka, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Koezuka, Ryu** M.P., ex-vice-Pres. of the House of Representatives, was b. '51 in Hyoyo-ken, studied under late Kei-u Nakamura and early took to journalism and political life in Tokyo, Dir. of Mining Bureau when the Kenseito Cabinet was formed in '98, then Governor of Tokyo which he resigned with fall of the Cabinet. Add. Hitotsugi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Koga, Renzo** Crown Member of the House of Peers, b. '58 in Saga; joined Eto's rebellion raised '74, was captured but acquitted with reprimand. After fighting against adversity in Tokyo '74-'80 he succeeded entering the defunct Law Col., Justice Dep't. and with his graduation '84 his position was assured. He was Public Procurator at various courts, ultimately sat on the bench of Supreme Court '98 and was transferred to Dir. Police Bureau of Home Office in '06, to resign it '08 with the fall of the Seiyukai Cabinet, and again to the same post in '11. Add. Yamabushi-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Koganei, Seiryo** M.D., and Prof. Med. Col. Imp. Tokyo Univ., his speciality being diseases of the brains. He was born in '58 at Nagacka; entered the Imp. (now Tokyo) Univ. '70; studied at Berlin '81-'85 and has been connected with the University every since. Add. Akefune-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Koike, Chozo** Dir. of Political Bureau since 1913, ex-Councillor to Japanese Embassy at London 1911-13, was b. '73 at Fukushima; passed the diplomatic and consular service examination in '96; attaché at Seoul in the same year, transferred to London in '97; personal Sec. to Foreign Minister in '00; Legation Sec. at Peking in '01, transferred to London in '02, Consul-General at New York, at San Francisco, at Mukden '08 and finally to the present post. Edu. Graduated from the Tokyo Imp. Univ. (Law) in '96. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Koike, Kunizo** Broker of the Tokyo Stock Exchange, Member of Tokyo Chamber of Commerce, b. in Yamanashi-ken in 1866. Began his career under Mr. Wakao, millionaire merchant of Yamanashi and engaged in the business of banking, silk export, and gold-mining. In 1898, just after the Sino-Japanese war, opened business on his own account as a stock broker in Tokyo and by strict integrity and deligence he succeeded in gaining the confidence of his clients; with the expansion of his business he organized it in 1907 as semipartnership firm under the name of Koike & Company. He was one of the Japanese business men, who, invited by the United Chamber of Commerce of the Pacific Coast, undertook a tour in the States in 1909. Add. Office, 2, Kabuto-cho; residence, Nakano-cho, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n148/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Koizumi, Masayasu** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 8th Army Div., b. '55 in Ibaragi prefecture; grad. from the defunct Mil. Non-Com. Sch., Sub-Lieut, '77; Colonel '77; Maj.-Gen. '03; promoted to the present rank in Aug. '10; fought in the China-Japan war as Brigade Commander and also in the late war, when he was wounded at the battles of Liaoyang and Saho. Decorated with 2nd class Rising Sun and Golden Kite (war honour). Add. Hirosaki, — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Koizumi, Yeitaro** Vice-Adm., Com. of Yokosuka Reserve Fleet; was b. in '65 in Tokyo, Midshipman '81; Captain '02; Rear-Adm., '08, was Divisional Commander of the Maisushima in the Japan-China war; was on the Committee charged to bring home the cr. Azuma from France in '01, Chief Staff to Takeshiki Naval Station '03-'04. Add. Yokosuka Admiralty. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kojima, Kenshi** B. Ar. (Cornell class '81), Prof. First High School since '86, and Mem. of the Teacher's Licence Committee; is reputed as English scholar. Add. Yumi-cho, 1/23, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Komatsu, Kenjiro** ex-Vice-Minister of Communications, and Crown Mem. of the House of Peers, was b. '63 in Nagano-ken, grad. from Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ., '88, and has been connected with the Department ever since, having been promoted to the present post in 1911 but resigned in Dec. '12. Was despatched abroad in '97 on official business; was severely wounded by a burglar in Sept. '11 in his official residence. Add. Tsukudo Hachiman, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Komatsu, Midori** Dir. Foreign Affairs Bureau, Korean Gov.-Gen., was b. '65 in Tokyo; grad. at Michigan Univ., '90; translator and Secretary of the Foreign Office till '07; transferred to Korean where he has risen to the present position. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Komatsu, Teruhisa** 2nd Lieut. (Navy), 1st Marquis, the House being newly created in '10 to succeed the House of the late Prince Komatsu who died without an heir; was b. '88, fourth son of the late Prince Kita-Shirakawa; grad. at Naval Academy '09. Add. Kioi-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Komatsubara, Yeitaro** ex-Minister of Education and Member of the House of Peers, b. in '52 at Okayama; took active part in democratic movement and was even imprisoned for some years; afterward appointed a Secretary of Foreign Affairs '81; next Secretary of the Lagation at Berlin; Vice-Minister of Justice, and next of Home Affairs Nov. '98-Sept. 1900; and afterwards entered the Osaka Mainichi Shimbun, but resigned the editorship in 1903; Minister of Education from '08 till '11. Add. Kudan-zaka, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kometa, Torao** Baron, Manager of Board of Chamberlains and Supt. of Board of Hunting, Imp. Household, Lieut-Col. (ret.) b. in 1839 in the province of Higo; took part in the Restoration War and served several Dept. as secretary; Court Councillor in '99; Supt. of Board of Hunting '99; and Board of Cham. in '12 as additional post. Add. Surugadai, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Komoto, Jujiro** D.M., b. '59; studied in Germany and Austria '85-'89; Prof. at the Medical Col. of Imp. Tokyo Univ., and is one of the most distinguished ophthalmologists of the day. Add. Fujimi-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n149/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Komura, Kin-ichi** Marquis (2nd), jun. Secretary of the Foreign Office, was b. in 1882, eldest son of the late Juaro Komura, 1st Marquis, one of the greatest diplomatists in recent Japan; grad. with honor from the Coll. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, 1907; passed the diplomatic Service Exam. the same year; attache at London '08-'10; transferred to the home office '11. Add. Haramachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kondo, Hisayoshi** Per. Secretary to the Imperial Household Minister and Court Councillor, was b. in 1852 in Choshu; entered the Household Department in '79, where he has remained ever since. Add. Nakacho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kondo, Jihan** M.D., Prof. at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., was b. in 1834 in Shinano; grad. from the Coll. of Medicine in 1891; further studied surgery at Strasburg and Heidelberg '95-'98. Add. Minami-Kogacho, Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kondo, Motoki** Inspector General of Naval Construction, Pres. of the Kogyoku-sha, Director of the Naval Experimental Section, Mem. of the Dep't of Materials, Navy, was b. '64 in Tokyo; is Director of the Kogyoku-sha, private school founded by his father who did much in the cause of naval education. Received Hon. Prize from Imperial Academy of Japan '13 Add. Hiratsuka-mura, Tokyo-Fu. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kondo, Rempei** Baron (cr. '11), President of the Nippon Yusen Kaisha (Japan Mail Steamship Co.), b. '50, and is long connected with shipping business, having served under the late Iwasaki whose relative by marriage he subsequently became; was appointed a Director on the creation of the present Co.; elected Vice-President at the time of the Japan-China War and finally its President; bears the 2nd Order of the Rising Sun in recognition of his service in transport business during the war. Was M.P. in the 1st session of the Diet, but has lately kept aloof from politics. Add. Ichigaya, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kono, Hironaka** M.P. of unbroken record and leader of the Doshikai was b. at Fukushima '49; entered early political career joined the democratic movement started by Count Itagaki, and is one of the pioneer Liberals who fought in the cause of freedom and suffered much from collisions with the authorities. Was the leader of the Fukushima trouble and thrown in prison for several years; seceded '93 from the Liberal Party to join its rivals, the Progressives, and at once sprang into notoriety for a short while when as President of the Lower House he invited its dissolution by submitting an impeachment Reply to the Throne soon after the opening of the session of '03; had previously seceded from the Progressives; was arrested on the charge of sedition in connection with the Anti-Peace violence of Sept. 1905, but was acquitted. Was a leader of the Kokuminto but left in Jan. 1913 and joined Prince Katsura's party. Add. Kobinata, Daimachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Konoike, Zenne-mon** Baron (cr. '11); is one of the oldest millionaire families in Japan, dating 3 centuries back; runs the Konoike Bank, and is at the same time the largest shareholder of the Nippon Life Insurance Co. and the Osaka Savings Bank. Add. Konoike, Osaka. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Koncye, Fumimaro** Prince, one of the proud Fujiwara houses and a gosecke (see Ichijo); was founded by Motozane (1143-66 A.D.) whose brother Kanezane (1148-1207) founded the three princely houses of Kujo, Nijo and Ichijo. The present head is still a minor and his father Atsumaru (1863-1904) was President of House of Peers. Add. Shibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n150/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Koyama, Junji** ex-Governor of Gumma, was b. in '70 at Kumamoto, graduated law at the Imperial Tokyo Univ. in '95 was clerk at the Home Office and next Secretary in the provinces and also in Tokyo, having held posts of Personal Secretary to the Minister of Justice and of Agriculture and Commerce, Director of Fishery Bureau in the latter Department in '06 and transferred to the present office in '08, resigned Mar. '13. Add. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kozai, Yoshinao** D. Agr. (Japan), Ph. D. (Leiptsic), Dean and Prof. of Coll. Agriculture, Imp. Univ., Tokyo; was b. 1865 in Kyoto-fu: grad. the Komaba Agr. Col. '85; Prof. at the alma mater '89-'95; Leipsic '95-'99, and resumed the duty at Komaba; Agriculturist to the Gov. Experimental Farm at Nishigahara '03 and then its chief '06; Dean in '11. Add. Nishigahara, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Katsumi** Dir. of the South Manchuria R'ly Co., 1905-'14, 3rd son of Denye-mon Higuchi, was b. '69 in Kagoshima; was adopted as son and heir by the late Mr. Kubota; graduated from Law Col. Imp. Univ. of Tokyo in '94; to be soon attached to the Bureau of National Treasury of the Nippon Ginko, and then transferred to the present post in '05. Add. Motomachi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Seishu** Governor of Tokyo, was b. '71 in Hokkaido; grad. Law, Imp. University of Tokyo in '95 and then entered the legal service as Public Procurator till '06 when he was appointed Governor of Yamagata, Dir. of South Manchuria R'ly, '06, Started for Europe in '08; on a tour of inspection of railway affairs, and returned home in '10; Governor of Miye in '11, Dir. of Eng. Bureau, and then of Public Works Bureau of the Home Dept., Dec '12—Apr. 14; present post in '14. Add. Official residence. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Seitaro** Judge, Adm. Litigation Court., b. '64 in Okayama; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '91; Sec. at local and the Home Office till '94; was sent abroad to investigate sanitary matters relating to factories '98-'99 and was transferred to be Secretary of Agr. and Com. to draw up draft of factory law, remaining as additional post Secretary of the Home Office; Dir. Sanitary Affairs Bureau '03-'10. Add. Fukide-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Yuzuru** Baron (cr. '07) ex-Minister of Education, Mem. House of Peers (since '94) b. '47 in Tajima; entered the civil service in connection with Education in which he became Vice-Minister '94-'94, and finally its chief, had to resign the Ministry Nov. '05 in connection with the University Prof.'s demonstration affair. Holds 1st class of the Order of Sacred Treasure. Add. Kanatomi-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kuga, Michihisa** Marquis, formerly courtier, the house being founded by Prince Tomohira, son of the Emperor Murakami; was b. at Kyoto, '41; appointed a commander of the Imp. Army at the time of the Restoration, Sec. of the Imperial Household and the Cabinet, then Governor of Tokyo '96-'97. Appointed Director of Board of Decorations in '09. Add. Shin-ogawa-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n151/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kuhara, Fusanosuke** Pres. of the Kuhara Mining Co. at Osaka, the lease of the Hidachi Copper Mine, etc., was b. 1869 at Osaka; grad. from Keiogijuku '89 ; a clerk at the Kosaka Copper Mine which was resuscitated by him from the threatened danger of decay. He was made the supervisor of the mine, and resigned the post 1898. Bought the Kuhara Mine 1905, and has exploited it as one of the best copper mines in Japan. Add. Nakanoshima, Osaka. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kujo, Michizane** Prince, one of the proud Fujiwara houses and formely a gossekke (see Ichijo); founded by Kanezane (1143-1207). The present head was b. 1869 and Ritualist on duty at Court, and one of his sisters is now the Empress. Add. Fukuyoshi-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kuki, Takakadzu** Baron, (cr. '05) Privy Councillor, b. '52 in Settsu; was for several years a high official in the Dep't of Education; Minister at Washington between '84 and '88. Director of the Imperial Nara Museum since '90, Exhibition Commissioner, etc., is a great virtuoso in Japanese painting. Add. 14 Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kuma, Tokuzo** Director of the Accounts Dep't. of the Kwantung Govt. was b. in Fukuoka in 1862, Sub-Lieutenant in 85, Lieutenant in '88, removed to the Accountant service in '93, Paymaster-Colonel in '05 and Paymaster-General in '09. Add. Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kume, Keiichiro** historian of the Western painting, b. '66 in Saga, son of the celebrated historian Kume Kunitake; studied in Paris '86-'93; appointed Professor of the Tokyo Fine Art School '96; is noted for landscape painting. Add. Iigura-Katamachi, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kume, Kinya** ex-Vice-Minister of the Dep't Agr. and Com., b. '67 in Tokyo; finished Law, Imperial Tokyo Univ., '86, and has since held post of Councillor or Secretary at the Communication Dep't in which he rose to be Dir. Communication Bureau, to be transferred to that of Patent Bureau '04 and then Dir. Forestry Bureau '06-'07. Resigned the post of Vice Min. in '08. Add. Nagazumi-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kume, Kunitake** D. Litt. (Japan), historian, Prof. Waseda Univ.; b 1844 in Saga; was on suite of the late Prince Iwakura's mission to America and Europe in 1872; was next in the compilation Bureau, Cabinet, and afterward Prof. of the 1st High School, but his article about the origin of the Japanese race obliged him to resign; has been connected with Waseda ever since. Add. Meguro, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kume, Ryosaku** Dir. of the Tokyo Gas Works since '07; was born '67 in Saitama; studied at the Keiogijuku and the Hogaku-in (Chuo Univ.) in Tokyo; was for years Managing Director of the Nippon Railway Co. before its nationalization. Add. Sendagi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kunisawa, Shimbei** Vice-President of the South Manchuria R'ly Co. since '06; was born in '64; a native of Tosa; grad. in '89 from Eng. Col. of Imp. Tokyo Univ.; engineer of the nationalized Kyushu R'ly Co. '89; served at the Railway Department '92-'05; was connected with the field railroads in Manchuria before the South M. R'ly Co. was created. Add. Dairen, Manchuria. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kurachi, Tetsukichi** Dir. of Chngokn Kogyo Co., b. '71 in Kanazawa, passed higher civil service examination in '96, Councillor of Foreign Office in '97; 3rd class Sec. at Berlin in '99, Councillor at head office '01 and Secretary of Agri. and Com. which latter he resigned in '03, attached to the Japanese Envoy to the Peace Conference at the Hague in '07, decorated with 4th class Rising Sun, 3rd class Sacred Treasure for his service in regards to the conclusion of conventions with France and Russian, and with 3rd class Rising Sun for the latter service. Vice-Min. of Foreign Dept., '11-'12, and decorated with 2nd class Rising Sun for the Annexation of Korea. Edu. Grad. from Tokyo Imp. Univ. (Law) in '94. Add. Harajiku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n152/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kuratomi, Yusaburo** Crown Men. of House of Peers and ex-Dir. of the Legislative Bureau '13-'14, was b. '53 in Fukuoka Prefecture; grad. the Law Coll., Justice Dept. '79; Judge '82; long held post of Councillor of the Judicial Dep't. Dir. Civil and Cri'l Bureau '98; Vice-Minister '99; Procurator of the Supreme Court, and Chi. Procurator of the Osaka and Tokyo Appeal Courts, Bureau Director and Vice-Minister of Justice; Vice-Min. of Justice of the Korean Gov. '07-'13. Add. Tango-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Kure, Bunso** ex-Chief statist of Dep't of Arg. and Com., b. '51 in Tokyo; clerk in the Cabinet '75 and has since been devoted to statistic work. Was sent abroad to U.S.A. and Europe 1900 to investigate statistic affairs. Is teaching his special science at various institutions. Add. Motozono-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Kure, Daigoro** Chief-Man. of the Mitsui Bussan, was b. '62 in Nagasaki; was sent to China by the Foreign Office in '77 to study the Chinese language; attaché to the Japanese Consulate at Tientsin '85; Chancellor to the Japanese Consulate at London '86; transferred to Bombay which post he soon resigned to enter the Mitsui Bussan. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Kure, Hidezo** M.D., Prof. at the Imp. Tokyo University, was b. in 1865 in Tokyo; grad. Medicine at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., 1890; sent to Germany for study, 1897-1900; is a specialist on insanity. Add. Sekiguchi-Daimachi, Koi. T. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Kurino, Shin-ichiro** Viscount, ex-Amb. to France, b. at Fukuoka '52; Commissioner of the Foreign Office '81; Junior Secretary '84; Vice-Chief of the Investigation Bureau '86; transferred to the Communication Dep't to act as Personal Secretary to its Minister, promoted to be Director of the Foreign Correspondence Bureau in the Dep't three years after; returned to the Foreign Office '91 as Director of the Investigation Bureau and then that of the Political Bureau. Next commenced his diplomatic career first as Minister at Washington '94; at Rome '96; at Paris '97; and at St. Petersburg '01, in the stormy time preceding the diplomatic rupture; appointed to the present post '06. Add. Hinoki-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Kuroda, Kiyoteru** 2nd Viscount, Prof. at the Gov. Art Academy since 1902, was b. 66; studied painting at Paris under Colin '84-'93, is one of the master painters of Western school. Add. Hirakawa-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Kuroda, Nagashige** Marquis (cr. '03, formerly Daimyo of Fukuoka), Vice-President of the Home of Peers, b. '67 at Fukuoka; studied at Cambridge Univ. '84-'89; a Master of Ceremonies '89-'90; is President of the Oriental Association. Add. Fukuyoshi-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Kuroiwa, Shuroku** Edi. and Prop. of the Yorozu Choho, b. '62 in Tosa; was long connected with various papers chiefly as translator of foreign novels: started the present daily in '92, and by his clever management created for it a wide circulation and to-day it is one of the best circulated journals. Add. Kogai-cho, Azaku, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Kuroki, Iten** Gen. (ret.) Count, (Baron '95, elevated '07), 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Class of Rising Sun and Paulownia (war honor), b. in Satsuma, '44; commanded the Kumamoto Army Division in the Japan-China war, and achieved a distinguished service at Weihai-wei under Marshal Oyama Feb. '95; promoted General in Nov. 1903; commander of the First Army in the late War and won the first great victory at the Battle of the Yalu, succeeded by the brilliant series of successful campaigns: appointed Mem. of the Supreme Military Council Dec. '95. Attended Jamestown Exhibition U.S.A. as representative of Japan. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Kurose, Yoshikado** Baron ('07), holder of Grand Cross of Rising Sun, Lieut-Gen. (ret.), b. '46; in Okayama; Ensign '71; was Commander of Artillery Corps of the 2nd Army in the Japan-China War and was rewarded 4th Order of Golden Kite; Lieut.-Gen. '61 and put on retired list the following year. He was commissioned Commander of Formosan Garrison in the late War. Add. Nando-cho, Ushigome, T. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Kuru, Masamichi** architect to Edu. Dep't, b. '58 at Shizuoka; grad. Arch. at the former Eng. College '81; appointed Arch. to Edu. Dep't '86 and this post he has held with only a short break, and was in charge of architectural section of Japan at the Centennial Exhibition and St. Louis Exhibition. He was Chief Architect in building the present Japan Bank building and several other edifices. Add. Edogawa, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Kurusu, Sobei** Vice-Pres. of Yokohama Chamber of Commerce, Mg. Dir. Yokohama Dock Co., etc., was b. in 1855 in Saitama-ken, and has risen to present position by starting export of raw silk at Yokohama when he was still young. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Kusakabe, Benjiro** Engineer, D. Eng. (Japan) Chief Engineer of the Tokyo City-Office; was b. '61 in Shiga-ken; grad. Eng. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '86; was long at the Home Office as its Chief Engineer and once been to Europe; received his doctorate in '01. Add. Hirakawa-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Kusakabe, Sankuro** ex-Dir. of For. Sec:, Gov.-Gen., Kwantung, b.
