**Tsubono, Heitaro** Dir. Tokyo Higher Commercial School, was b. '59 in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. Col. Law, Imperial Tokyo Univ. '85, was in the consular and postal services till 98, banker till '01, Dir. of the Kobe Commercial School and next Mayor of the City till '06; Dir. Yamaguchi Higher Com. School till '11 when he was transferred to Tokyo. Add. Tokyo, Higher Commercial School. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Tsubouchi, Yuzo** D. Litt (Japan), author and a man of letters, was b. '59 in Aichi-ken; is connected with the Waseda Univ. from its inception, '82, he having grad. that year Co. Lit. of the now Imp. Tokyo Univ., is more widely known in public as the pioneer novelist of modern education, is the foremost Shakespearean Scholar of Japan and translated several of his plays; has also writtten several dramas and works on ethics; the Literature and Drama Society organized in '11 but dissolved it in '13. Add. Okubo near Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Tsuboya, Zenshiro** Dir. of Hakubunkan, Mem. Tokyo City Council, journalist and travellers, b. '62 in Echigo; grad. from Waseda '88, and connected with the Firm ever since; toured through Europe '10; has written a number of works on travel, etc. Add. Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Tsuchiko, Kinshiro** Vice-Pres. of the Yokohama Fire and Transport Insurance Co., b. '63 at Mino, grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '85; studied for several years in America and England, and went through practical side of banking business as an extra-employé of a certain bank in America; entered the Yokohama Specie Bank about '90 and held post of junior agent at its London Branch; entered the present Co., '96. Add. Yumi-cho, Hongo, T. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Tsuchiya, Kokin** Rear-Adm. Com. of the 3rd Squadron, was b. in Okazaki in 1865, eldest son of Gen. Baron Tsuchiya; Midshipman '86, Commander '00, Captain '05, Rear-Adm. and Com. of Maizuru Torpedo Corps '11, Com. of the Kure Adm. Squadron '12-'14. Add. Kure. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Tsuchiya, Mitsuharu** Baron (cr. '07), Gen. (ret.), b. in 1854 in Mikawa; early entered Military service and app. Sub-Lieut. in '72; took part in Japan-China War as Staff Officer of Headquarters, and also Com. of 11th Army Div. in late Russo-Japanese War and wounded at Port Arthur; rewarded with 1st Order of Rising Sun and 2nd merit of Golden Kite. Add. Okubo near Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Tsuchiya, Tamotsu** Vice-Adm., ex-Chief of the Naval Personnel Bureau, was b. in Tokyo-fu, '50; appointed Sub-Lieut. in '85; Mem. of the 1st Section of the Naval Staff Board and the Bureau for Naval Affairs; Rear-Adm. '07; Com. of Ominato Station '12. Add. Dote-Sanban-cho, Kojima-chi, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Tsuda, Umeko** daughter of the late Tsuda Sen, an agriculturist, b. '65 at Tokyo; was among the first batch of girls sent '71 to U.S.A. for study by the Imp. Gov., stayed there till '82, and on returning home was appointed teacher in the Peeress School and lecturer in the Women's Higher Normal School; is the proprietor of the Girls' English School founded by herself in Kojimachi. Has frequently visited America. Add. Goban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n214/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Tsuji, Shinji** Baron (cr. '08), Mem. House of Peers, Pres., Education Society, b. was '42 in Shinano; from '66 till he retired in '93 as Vice-Minister. he was in the service of Education Depar't. Add. Yumicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsujimura, Kusuzo** Paymaster and Director of the Account and Supply Bureau of the Army; b. in '65 in Kochi; graduated with honour from the Military Paymasters' School in '93; was Paymaster to the Guards' Division and instructor of the Paymaster School before he was transferred to the central Office in '02; subsequently appointed Pres. of the Paymasters' Sch., Chief Paymaster of the 10th Army Division, Garrison of Liaotung, Guards' Division, etc.; promoted to the present office in Aug. '09. Add. Ichigaya Nakano-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsukahara, Osamu** journalist and novelist, b. '48 as Tokyo; was connected with the Nichi Nichi from '78, but now on the Tokyo Nichi Nichi; is reputed as one of the foremost writers of romance. Add. Kioi-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsukamoto, Katu-yoshi** Baron, Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), b. '47 in Gifu; Sub-Lieut. '72, has subsequently served as Reg. Com., Dir. Military Coll., Chief of Staff, 9th Division and took part in '94-'95 and '04-'05 Wars. Add. Kanagawa. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsukamoto, Yasushi** Prof. of Architecture, Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. '69 in Kyoto; grad. in '93 from University of which he was appointed lecturer; further studied in England, Germany and France '99-'02; Prof. at the alma mater on his return. Add. Hisakata, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsukuda, Ichiyo** Dir. of the South Manchurian Rly. (app. 1913), ex-Vice-Pres. of the Yokohama Specie Bank, was b. '68 in Ehime-ken; grad. Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90, and subsequently entered the Finance Dept. in which he served as Secretary, Chief of Customs House, etc.; Adv. to Yuan Shinkai '03-'06. Add. Harajiku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsukui, Shigeru** Dir. of Stock and Shares Bureau, Bank of Japan; was b. 1869 in Hyogo-ken; grad. from Imperial Tokyo Univ. (Law), 1893, and for some years practised law. Add. Ichigaya Kaga-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsumagi, Rai-o** Dr. Eng., ex-Dir.-Gen. Construction Bureau, Finance Dep't., Vice-President of Japan Architecture Society, was b. in Tokyo; studied architecture '78-'82 at the Engineering Col., Tokyo, then at Cornell Univ., U.S.A. class '84; architect to Home Office '80; sent to Germany to investigate his speciality '86-'90, to resume duty at the Home Office on returning home; transferred to the Treasury '96 to take charge of construction affairs (resign in '13); dispatched to Europe and America in '01; built the Yokohama Specie Bank (plan of which won gold medal at the Brussels World's Fair), Tokyo City Office, Tokyo Chamber of Commerce and many others. Add. Dai-machi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Tsuruhara, Sadakichi** M.P. (Seiyukai), Pres. Japan Manure Co., ex-Dir.-Gen. of Res.-Gen. Korea ('05-'08), was b. '55. Law, at the Imp. Tokyo Univ, '83, and entered the Foreign Office where he was rapidly promoted and was appointed Consul at Shanghai and also at London; entered the Bank of Japan where he filled the post of Dir. Banking Bureau; resigned the post '98, Mayor of Osaka '02-'05. Add. Naka-rokuban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n215/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Tsuruta, Shikakichi** Surgeon-General of the Fleets and Hospitals (retired); b. '57 in Yanagawa, Kyushu; 2nd Assist.-Surgeon of the Navy '81; Surgeon-in-Chief of the Naval Academy, and of Yokosuka and Kure Admiralties; Dir. of the Yokosuka Naval Hospital; Chief of Admiralties; Pres. of the Naval Medical College; Sectional Chief of the Naval Med. Dept.; placed on the retired list in '11. Add. Mita-Koyama-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Tsuzuki, Keiroku** Baron (cr. '08), Privy Councillor since '07, was b. '62 at Tokyo; studied in Germany '82-'85, after grad. from the Imperial Tokyo University; appointed Sec. of Legation and Per. Sec. to the Foreign Min., '76; travelled abroad '89 on the suite of Prince Yamagata, entered the Home Office in which he subsequently appointed Dir. of Public Works Bureau, '94; again travelled with Prince Yamagata when the latter was dispatched to Moscow to attend the Tsar Nicholas' coronation ceremony; Vice-Min. of Edu., '07; Env. Extr. Min. Plen. unattached '98; Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs '99 under the late Vis. Aoki but left it owing to some disagreement of views with his chief. Was on the suite of the late Prince Ito when he visited Europe, '01-'02. Appointed as Special Amb. to attend The Hague Peace Conference of '07; was created Baron for the service; is a son-in-law of Marquis Inouye. Add. Mamiana, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uchida, Kakichi** Dir. Civil Affairs, Formosa (app. Aug. '10), b. '66 in Tokyo; graduated Law, Imperial Tokyo Univ. '91, after which he entered the Department of Communications in which he held post of Dir. of Shipping Bureau till '10. Add. Taipeh, Formosa. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uchida, Sadatsuchi** Minister in Sweden since '11, was b. '65 at Kokura; appointed attaché in '89; éleve Consul at Shanghai in '90; transferred to Seoul in '03, Consul-General in '02; Min. and Con-General in Brazil in '07; decorated with 3rd Class Rising Sun with grant of money in '06 for his service during the War. Grad. Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '89. Add. Jap. Legation, Sweden. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uchida, Yasuya** Viscount (Baron '07, Vis. '11) ex-Minister of Foreign Affairs, was b. '65 in Kumamoto-ken; was attaché of the Legation at Washington '87; Per. Sec. to the Min. of Agr. Com. (C't Mutsu), '90; held a similar post at the Foreign Office when his chief was transferred to it; Sec. of Legation at London '93; transferred to a similar post at Peking '95-'98; Dir. of Political Bureau, to be promoted Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs '00 and removed to the Legation at Peking, '01-'06; nominated Amb. at Vienna in Feb. '07; Amb. to U.S.A. '09 and to the Minister in '11, resigned in '12. Mrs. Uchida is a daughter of Mr. Dogura, a millionaire of Yamato, and was edu. at Vassar Col., U.S.A. Add. Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uchimura, Kanzo** Christian teacher and essayist, was b. '61 in Saitama; grad. Sapporo Agr. Col. '81; clerk at the Dep't of Agr. and Commerce, studied at Amherst Univ. '83-'88; instructor at the Higher School, Tokyo, and by his refusal to bow before the Emperor's portrait from religious scruple he had to resign; has subsequently devoted himself to journalism, educationist and non-Sectarin Christian teacher, and is publishing or religious monthly. Wrote "How I became a Christian" and others. Add. Tsunohazu, near Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uchimura, Tatsujiro** Mech., E., Mem., A.S.R.E., leading Patent Solicitor, was b. '67 in Tokyo; grad. from the Tokyo Higher Technical College in '90 and was examiner of the Gov't Patent Bureau till '06, when he resigned to become chief expert of the Imp. Refrigerating Co., travelled through Europe and America to inspect cold storage business in '07; is the first introducer and erector of cold storage and refrigerating plant in Japan, and has also done much for Japanese fishery by applying Western mode and systems. He is Director of the Industrial-Improvement Association and of the Imperial Inventional Association. Add. Yariya-cho, Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n216/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Uchiyama, Kojiro** Lieut.-Gen., Chief Aide-de Camp to his Majesty since 1912, and Commander of the 12th Army Division (at Kokura), b. '59 in Tottori; Sub-Lieut. (Art.) '70; attached to the Legations in Russia and France; rewarded with 4th Class Golden Kite in the Japan-China War, and was Com. Art. Corps attached to the 2nd Army during the late War; attached to the 2nd Army during the late War; attache to Japanese Legation at St. Petersburg till '07; Commander of Yura Fort and of Toyohashi Div. '08. Add. Daimachi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Uemura, Chozaburo** Mng. Dir. Japan Beer Brewery Co., etc., was b. 1862 in Tokyo; was for some years an official of the Departments of Agr. and Com. and of Communications. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Uemura, Masahisa** a noted Christian preacher and Prof. at the Meiji Gaku-in, was b. 1859 in Tokyo; is one of the prominent Christians brought up by the late Dr. Neejima who remain staunch believers; pastor of the Fujimicho Church. Add. Nakarokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Ukita, Kazutami** D.C.L. (Japan), historian and educationalist, Prof. Waseda Univ., Chief Editor of the Taiyo (monthly magazine published by the Hakubunkan), was b. '60 in Kumamoto; studied English under Capt. James of U.S.A. engaged by the Lord of the Fief and was converted into a Christian; entered the Doshisha '76 and grad. it '79 was for some while engaged in religious work and journal; Prof. at his alma mater; entered Yale '92 and studied philosophy and politics; was again Prof. at the Doshisha and next at present institution '97. Add. Zoshigaya, near Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Umezawa, Michiharu** Lieut.-Gen., Commander of the 6th Division (Kumamoto) was b. '53 in Miyagi-ken; Sub-Lieut. '72; Colonel in '99; Maj.-Gen. in '05 and to the present rank of Lieut.-Gen. in '11; took part both in the China-Japan War and the late war in which gallant feat at Penchihu, in the battle of Mukden was highly reputed. Attended Jamestown Exhibition, U.S.A. in '03. Add. Kumamoto Division. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Uno, Hogara** Doctor and Prop. of the Rakuzindo Hospital for skin diseases, was b. in Shidzuoka in '50; grad. Medicine at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '76; Assist.-Prof. in the Col. in '81, and subsequently appointed Vice-Pres. of University Hospital; was sent to Germany in '89 for study. Resigned the chair in '01 to establish the hospital. Add. Kojima-cho, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Uryu, Shin** Dir. of the Kirin Beer Brewing Co., Auditor of the Tokyo Warehouse Co., etc., was b. '53 in Fukui-ken; first studied English at Nagasaki and then at Tokyo in '67; was on suite of late Ambassador, Prince Iwakura; was made to stay in Europe '72-75 to be made an official of the Railway Bureau on returning home; resigned it in '77 got into the service of the Mitsubishi firm. Add. Kami-rokuban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n217/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Uryu, Sotokichi** Baron (cr. '07), Admiral, Mem. Admiral's Council, b. '54 at Kanazawa; entered the Navy early and was dispatched to Annapolis to complete study; Cap., '91; naval attache at the French Legation till '96; appointed Commander of the Akitsushima, Fuso, Yashima and others; Rear-Adm. '00 with a post at the Naval Staff Board; on the outbreak of the late war, commanded a detachment under Adm. Togo and sank the Yaryag and Koreetz at Ninsen on Feb. 9th; took part in the battle of Aug. 14th (off Shantung) and in that of the Japan Sea, meanwhile promoted Vice-Adm. and appointed Commander of Takeshiki, and next Sasebo, of which he was relieved in '00 and made Member of the Admirals' Council. Com'der Yokosuka Admiralty '09-'10; full Adm. and placed on retired list in Dec. '12. He and his wife, who was educated in America, attended the Annapolis anniversary function in '09. Add. Kanasugi, Nippori, near Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Usagawa, Kazumasa** Baron (cr. '07), Lieut.-Gen. (retired), ex-Pres. of Oriental Development Co., Seoul (1908-13), was b. '49 in Yamaguchi; 4th son of T. Fujimura and adopted by K. Usagawa of the clan; Lieut. '89, staff officer to the 1st Army in the Japan-China War which gained him the 4th Class Golden Kite; Major-Gen. '01 and promoted '06. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Usami, Shofu** Dir. Internal Affairs, Korean Residency Gen., was b. '69 in Yamagata; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class '96; was in the service of the Home Office at provinces and the head office, appointed Gov. of Toyama in '80; transferred to the Residency-Gen. '00. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Utsunomiya, Taro** Lieut.-Gen., Dep'tal Chief of the General Staff Board, was b. in Saga '61; Sub-Lieut. of Infantry in '85; Col. '05 and Major-Gen. in '80. Was once attached to the Japanese Legation in London '04. Add. Sendagaya, Harajuku, Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Uyeda, Bin** Man of letters and Prof. in the Lit., Col., Imp. Univ., Kyoto, was b. '74 in Shidzuoka; grad. the English Lit. Course in the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '97; Prof. in the Tokyo Higher Normal School in '99, to be transferred to the present post in '09; is an authority on English Literature. Undertook a tour through Europe in '08-'09. Add. Imperial Univ., Kyoto. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Uyeda, Kisaburo** Consul-General at Sydney since Sept. '07, b. '64, in Akita-ken; clerk at San Francisco and Berlin in '92-'95; Consul at Hongkong in '07; transferred to San Francisco in '06; grad. from the Tokyo Higher Com. School in '90. Add. Japan Consulate, Sydney. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Uyeda, Mannen** Philologist, Dean and Prof. Lit. Col. Imp. Tokyo Univ., was b. '67 in Aichi-ken, grad. Lit. Col. of the Univ. in '88; sent to France and Germany to prosecute further study; was once appointed acting Dir. of the Tokyo Foreign Language School; Councillor and Bureau Chief of the Educational Dep't. Add. Muko-Yanagiwara, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Uyeda, Yutaku** Baron (cr. '07), General (ret.), ex-Commander of Korean Garrison, was b. 1850 in Tokushima; Captain '72, chief staff of the 5th Division in the Japan-China war; Maj.-Gen. '97, and Dir. of Staff Col., Lieut.-Gen. '02; Chief of the Staff at the Military Inspection Board, full Gen. in '12; took part in the late war as Com. of 5th Division; Com. of 7th Division '07; Com. of Guards Div. '09-'11, placed on retired list in '12. Add. Yoyohata near Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n218/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Uyehara, Yusaku** Baron (cr. '97), ex-Minister of War (12-13), Lieut.-Gen., Commander of the 4th Division (Osaka), b. '56, in Hyuga; Sub-Lieut. (Eng.) '79; was ordered to study in France '89; staff officer to the 1st Army in the Japan-China War which gained him 4th Class Golden Kite; appointed Com. of 7th Division, attended the coronation ceremony of the Tsar and also The Hague Peace Conference; chief staff to Marshal Nozu in the War '08; Chief, Mil. Education '14. Add. War Office. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Uyemura, Masahisa** Prof. of Meiji Gakuin and pastor, was b. '57 in Tokyo; is one of the few remaining faithful followers of the late Dr. Niijima, founder of the Doshisha and father of Japanese Christianity. Add. Naka-rokuban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Uzawa, Somei** D.C.L. (Japan), M.P. (Seiyukai), barrister, was b. '72, in Chiba; where his precocious ability attracted wide attention; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class 99, and has been practising since then, being reputed as an expert in criminal cases. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Wada, Eisaku** painter of Western style, Prof. in the Tokyo Fine Art Acad., was b. '74 in Kagoshima-ken; grad. from the Academy in '96 and after having served as Assist.-Prof. at his alma mater for some years he was sent to France where he studied under Raphael Collin; is a Mem. of the hanging Committee of the Imp. Art Society. Add. Kasumi-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Wada, Hikojiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers ('11), was b. '59 in Hiroshima-ken; took active part in the democratic movement under Count Itagaki about 1880; visited Europe and America, '89-'90; member of the former Nationalist Association; appointed Dir., Agr. Bureau, Dept., Agr. and Com., transferred to the Com. and Ind. Bureau, and then raised to be Vice-Minister; the Chief Commissioner of the Grand Exhibition '08; resigned in '11. Add. Kobiki-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Wada, Kensuke** Rear-Adm., ex-Chief of the Naval Yard, Yokosuka, was b. in '61 in Kogoshima; Midshipman in '81; Captain '02; Commander of the cr. Naniwa in the late war; Rear-Adm. in '08; and placed on retired list '12. Add. Yokosuka Admiralty. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Wada, Toyoji** Man. Dir. of the Fuji Spinning Co., eldest son of Kunroku Wada, was b. '61 in Oita-ken; proceeded to America as Agent of the Kai Shoten about '99 and returned home about five years later; entered the Mitsui Bussan Kaisha, and Kanegafuchi Spinning Co., which was started in '96. Add. Mukojima, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Wadagaki, Kenzo** Economist, D.C.L. (Japan), noted English scholar and Prof. in the Agr. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ., Principal of Japan Girls' Commercial School, etc., was b. '60 in Hyogo-ken; grad. Lit. Col. of the Univ. in '80; further studied Political Economy at Cambridge, England, in '81; and afterward in Germany; visited Europe 1912-13. Add. Kohinata Dai-machi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Wakatsuki, Reijiro** Minister of Finance, was b. '66 in Shimane, grad. Law of the Imp. Univ., '92; was appointed Councillor of the Treasury, soon after graduation, where he rapidly rose and was appointed to be made a Bureau Dir. '03, and then to be the Financial Agent in London in '06; recalled home and appointed for the second time Vice-Minister of Finance '08 and resigned in '11 ; Minister Dec. '12—Feb. '13 and again in April '14—. Add. Naka-rokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n219/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Watanabe, Chiaki** ex-Minister of Imperial Household, was b. '43 at Suwa; was clerk at a local office '69; Secretary and then Governor at Kagoshima and others till about 1900; Gov. of Shiga then Hokkaido '01; Vice-Min. of the Household '02; Gov. of Kyoto '04-'05; Treasurer of the Household '05; Vice-Min. '08 and Privy Councillor Minister of the Household '10-'14. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Chifuyu** Man. Dir. of the Franco-Japanese Bank (Tokyo Branch), ex-M.P., son of the Count, adopted as heir by his uncle, Viscount Kunitake Watanabe, b. '76; grad. from Law, Imperial University (Tokyo) '98; further educated in France, Germany and America; Manager of Industrial Bank of Japan for some years; then Man. Director of Nippon Seikosho and Hokkaido Colliery and Steamship Co.; was for a while proprietor and editor of a Tokyo daily. Add. Nishimachi, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Chiharu** Count (cr. Baron '00, Vis. '07, Count '11), Manager of the Bank of Japan, was b. in 1872 in Tokyo; grad. from Coll. Literature, Imp. Tokyo, University 1895, and succeeded in 1914 the title of his father. He has been connected with the Bank since his graduation. Add. Takanawa Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Kaichi** Pres. of the Osaka-Kyoto Electric E'ly., was b. in Shinano '53; grad. in '83 Gov't Engineering College; conferred C.E. and B.S. by Glasgow Univ. which he finished in '86, then studied practical side under Sir John Fowler and other noted British engineers; elected member of Civil Eng'ing Society, London. Add. Shin-machi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Kunitake** Viscount (cr. '95), ex-Minister of Finance, b. '46, in Nagano-ken, was long connected with the Treasury till he rose to be the Vice-Min. '89-'92 and next to the principal post '92-'94; was temporarily transferred to the chair of Communications, but soon restored to the original chair of Finance which he left '95; was Finance Min. in the late Prince Ito's party Ministry in '00, but the Ministerial rupture over the question of financial adjustment and the retrenchment insisted upon by the Viscount culminated in the miserable collapse of the Cabinet. The Viscount is a great votarist of the Buddhist doctrine of asceticism, and wrote a little work on Indian philosophy. Add. Hommura-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Osamu** M.P. (Seiyukai), Dir. of Osaka Electric Lamp Co., etc., was b. in 1859 in Ehime; educated at the Keiogijuku; has held the post of Councillor in the Foreign Office; Mayor of Sasebo, etc. Add. Hayabusa-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Senjiro** Man.-Dir., Mitsui Bussan, b. '58 in Tokyo, a son of a doctor; studied commerce at Mr. Yano's Commercial School (forerunner of present Higher Com. School) and the now defunct Mitsubishi Com. School; entered the Mitsui Bussan '79, to be sent soon afterward to its London Branch, of which he had charge for over 20 years till his return home '02, to take post at the head office and again London Director in '07. Mrs. Watanabe is an English lady. Add. Mitsui Bussan, London. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Wataru** Dean of Eng. Col., Imp. Univ., Tokyo, b. '57 in Nagasaki; studied metallurgy in the Univ. (class '79); further studied at Freiburg, returning home in '75; had charge of Sado gold mine when it belonged to the Court, and then back to the alma mater. Add. Sendagi-Hayashi-cho, H., T. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n220/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Watase, Shozaburo** Ph. D.; D. Sc. (Japan) Prof. of Zoology, College of Science, Imperial University of Tokyo; grad. Imperial College of Agriculture Sapporo, 1884; studied Zoology at Imp. Univ. Tokyo, '84-'86; Johns Hopkins Univ. '86-'00; Clark Univ. '90-'92. Was Lecturer at the University of Chicago, 1892-1900; travelled extensively in America, Europe and in Tropics. Add. 9, Minami-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Watase, Torajiro** elder brother of the above, was b. at Numazu '60; grad. Sapporo, Agr. Col. '84. Was connected with educational work at Tokyo and in the provinces; then started business as seed-grower and importer and exporter of agricultural instruments and requisities. Sits in the Municipal Council, in which he has charge of the Grand Exhibition Section. Add. Tame-ike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yagyu, Kazuyoshi** Pres. of the Formosan Bank, was b. in 1864 at Nagoya; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. 1891; secretary to the Treasury '91-'00; Vice-Pres. of the Formosan Bank 1900-1912; present position 1912. Add. Taipeh, Formosa. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Chuzaburo** Lieut-Gen. and Commander of the 14th Army Division since 1912, was b. 4th in 1857 in Kyoto; graduated from the Military Academy in 1875, Lieutenant 1882 Captain 1887; Major 1893; Colonel 1902; Major-General, 1905. He has been Principal of the Sendai Military Preparatory School, Commander of the 11th Infantry Regiment, the 1st Infantry Regiment of the Body Guards, and of the 10th Infantry Brigade, President of the Toyama Gakko etc.; took part in the Russo-Japanese War as a Regimental Commander. Add. Utsunomiya, Tochigi-ken. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Eitaro** Mg. of the Iwakura Railway School, Councillor of Waseda Univ., Nisshin Life Insurance Co., Ltd., etc., was b. in 1862 in Aichi-prefecture; graduated from the Aichi Normal School; and afterwards from the Tokyo Semmon Gakko (forerunner of the present Waseda Univ.); took to journalism; entered business in 1892 and was connected with the Tokyo Electric Railway, the Narita Railway, and the Kinki Railway, the Nippon Railway Co., Ltd. and Official Receiver of the Nippon when it was nationalized. Add. Nakarokuban-cho, Koj., T. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Hikohachi** Vice-Admiral, Commander of the Yokosuka Admiralty, was b. in Kagoshima '55; began his career in the Navy when young, and was promoted to the Com. of the 1st Squad. in '08 and the present post in Dec. '12. Was honoured with 3rd Class of Golden Kite. Add. Higashi-Goken-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Suburo** Prof. in the Law Col. of the Imp. Toky Univ., was born '70 in Nara-ken; first studied at the Tokyo Semmon Gakko (now Waseda Univ.) and after grad. from it entered the special course and next the main course of the law Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. which he finished in '96. Further studied International Private Law at the Univ. Hall and in Germany, England, etc. '97-'01, to be appointed Prof. at the Univ. on his return; is also Prof. in the Waseda Univ. and is editor of a law magazine. Add. Benten-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Shin-ichiro** ex-Departmental Chief of the Formosan Gov't, was b. '64 in Fukuoka-ken; grad. Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90; entered the Dep't of Home Affairs; Governor of Tottori '03-'06; appointed to the Formosan post in '08; relieved in '10. Add. Shinsaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n221/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Yamagata, Aritomo** Marshal, Prince (Count '84; Marquis '95, Prince '07), Pres. of Privy Council, holder of Grand Cordon, O. M. (Br.), one of the elder statesmen, was b. '38 in Choshu, and took a distinguished part in the civil strife attending the Restoration; appointed Major-Gen. and a junior Vice-Minister of War by the Imp. Government; Lieut.-Gen., '72 and the Minister of War '73; was chief of Staff of the Army of Subjugation commanded by the late H.I.H. Prince Arisugawa in the civil war '77. After having sat frequently in the chair of Chief of the General Staff and of War, circumstances obliged him to undertake various civil posts, chiefly connected with Home Affairs and Justice Department. Visited Europe and America, '88-'89 to study the local self-government system and the constitution of civil corporations; commander of the 1st Army in the Japan-China War, but soon compelled by ill-health to return home. Attended the coronation ceremony of Tsar Nicholas '96, and brought home the Yamagata-Lobanow Convention in regard to Korea. Formed a Ministry '89-'91, and had rather stormy time with the freedom-loving people's representatives, and also in his second Ministry '08-'00. The Marshal's political career outwardly ended with the second Premiership, but he still continues to wield potential power in active politics, for the late Prince Katsura was believed as the Prince's successor in politics. On the outbreak of the late War the Marshal succeeded Marshal Oyama as Chief of the General Staff, to be transferred to the Presidency of Privy Council on the late Prince Ito's departure for Korea as Resident-Gen. Add. Sekiguchi, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamagata, Isaburo** Deputy-Res. Gen., Korea, ex-Minister of Communications, Crown Mem. House of Peers, was b. '57 in Choshu an adopted son and heir of Marshal Prince Yamagata, being a son of his sister. Studied in France; held various subordinate posts in the Central Government and as Governor in several provinces; Minister of Communications soon after the formation of the Saionji Ministry; resigned the chair early '08; appointed to present post '10. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamaguchi, Einosuke** D. Sc. (Japan) Director of the Imperial Archives and Mausolea, was b. '63 in Shimane-ken; grad. Physics at the Science Col. of the Tokyo Univ. in '84; Prof. in the First Higher Sch., and then in the Imp. Kyoto Univ. '97; President of the Peers' Sch. in '05; transferred to the present in '07. Add. Iidamachi, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamaguchi, Sogi** Dir. of the Bank of Japan, was b. '51 in Shimane-ken. Had long served in the Financial Dep't as Bureau Dir., and entered the Nippon Ginko about twelve years ago. He is elder brother of Mr. Einosuke Yamaguchi. Add. Haraikata-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamaji, Yakichi** a journalist, historian and essayist, was b. 1866 in Tokyo; has been on the staff of the Kokumin, to which he is a regular contributor. Add. Nakashibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamakawa, Gitaro** Prof. of Electric Engineering at the Imp. Univ., Tokyo, since '87, b at Kawagoe in '60; graduated electricity from the Gov. Engineering Col. in '82; visited Europe for study '96-'99; has written several works on his speciality. Add. Sendagi-Hayashi-cho, H., Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n222/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yamakawa, Kenjiro** D. Sc (Japan), President Imperial Tokyo Univ. Mem. House of Peers. b. '54 at Aizu, studied physics in Europe and America, was connected with the Tokyo Imp. Univ. after his return in '72, ultimately rising to the President; resigned it '05 dissatisfied with interference of executive authorities (v. "Tomizu"). Since '06 he had charge of the Tech. Col. founded near Wakamatsu, by Mr. Yasukawa millionair coal-miner; present post '13. Add. Hatsune-cho, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yamakawa, Yuki** Director and General Manager of the Yokohama Specie Bank, b. '55 in Ishikawa-ken; an official of the Treasury '76-'78; entered the Specie Bank in '80 and has continued in its service, having had charge of the Kobe Branch and of London Branch at the time of Russo-Japanese War. Add. Yokohama Specie Bank, Yokohama. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Gombei** ex-Premier, Mem. of Supreme Mil. Council Admiral (ret.), Count (cr. Baron '02, Count '07), b. '52 in Satsuma and fought in the War of the Restoration on the Imperial side; one of the prioneer graduates of the Naval Academy, class '77; was one board a naval training vessel that visited America and then made a voyage round the world on board a German warship; promoted Lieut. '80, and appointed Vice-Commander of the cruiser Asama; was one of the commissioners sent to England, '85 to bring home the cruiser Naniwa, built there; Commander of the g.b. Amagi, '86; was dispatched to Europe and America in '87 on suite of the then Vice-Minister of the Navy (Adm. Kabayama); Captain '90 and Commander of the Takao, and next that of the Takachiho. He was soon transferred to the Naval Office first as Personal Secretary to the Minister; Director of a Bureau and was gazetted Rear-Admiral, '95; Vice-Minister '96, then Vice-Adm. and Minister from '98 to Jan. '06, full Admiral, '04. Dispatched to England and U.S.A. on suite of Prince Fushimi '07 and was decorated with G.C.M.G. by King Edward; formed cabinet '13-'14 succeeding 3rd Katsura Ministry. The Naval Scandal involved his cabinet in great difficulty and it fell, in 1914. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Jotaro** ex-Executive Dir. of the Mitsui Bussan, was b. in 1867 in Fukui, entered the service of the Company in '82 and was attached to the Branch at Shanghai when he soon rose to its chief and remained till '04, transferred to the head office in 1912. Shimmachi, Ak., Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Kametaro** leading tea-exporter in Kobe, Pres. Kobe Chamber of Commerce etc., b. '61 in Hyogo-ken; has done much for promoting export trade of tea in the Kwansai district and this service has been recognized by the Government which has granted on him decorations. Sat at the International Congress of Com. at Washington, U.S.A. in '99. Add. Kaigandori, Kobe. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yamomoto, Tatsuo** Mem. House of Peers, ex-Gov. of Hypothec Bank of Japan, b. '56 in Oita-ken; connected with educational work from '80 to '82 when he entered the Mitsubishi Firm and was appointed a Director of the Yusen Kaisha, formed by amalgamation of the Mitsubishi with the rival company Kyodo; entered in '90 the Bank of Japan in which he was appointed Dir. of Banking Bureau, to be nominated Governor '98; relieved of the post '03; Gov. of Hypothec Bank of Japan '10; appointed to the Minister of Finance in '11, resigned in '12; again Minister of Agr. and Com. '13-'14. Add. Kami-Niban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yamanaka, Nobuyoshi** Baron, Lieut.-Gen. and ex-Commander of 18th Army Division at Kyoto, was b. '52 at Yamaguchi, Sub-Lieutenant in '71, Captain in '73, Major in '80, Lieut.-Colonel in '98, Colonel in '94, Major-General in '99, Lieut.-General in '05, was created a Peer in '70 in consideration of his service in the late war; placed on ret. list in Dec. '12. Add. Kyoto. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n223/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Yamanaka, Rinnoruke** Dir. Tokyo Card-Board, Fuji Paper Mill, etc., was b. '40 in Saitama; M.P. in the 1st Session, but has since kept aloof from politics. Add. Nakarokuhan-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yamane, Buryo, Baron** Lieut.-Gen. and Commander of Guard Division, was b. '04 at Yamaguchi; Sub-Lieutenant of Engineering in '77, Captain in '84, Major in '90, Colonel in '95 and was appointed chief adjutant at 6th and 12th Divisions, Major-Gen. '00 and Commander of Sasebo Fort, Lieut.-Gen. in '06 and Commander of Shimonoseki Fort, Baron in '07, Com. 8th Div., in '08; transferred to the present post in '12. Add. Aoyama, Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yamanouchi, Ichiji** Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '14) and ex-Chief Sec. of Cabinet, was b. in Kagoshima in '65; grad. Law (Imp. Tokyo Univ.) in '88, Secretary and next Section Chief of the Police Bureau (Home Office), Governor of Aomori '02-'4, Dir. of the R'ly Bureau '04-'6, Vice-Pres. of the Imp. R'lys., and then Director of R'ly. Adm. Dept., '0[illegible]-'12; Governor of Hokkaido '12. Add. Nagata-cho, Koj., T. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yamanouchi, Katsu-aki** Secretary and Ritualist at the Imperial Court, was b. '54 in Tokyo; entered the service of the Foreign Office in '73 but subsequently transferred to the Household Department where he was attached to the Cookery and other Sections, and finally promoted to the present posts. Add. Suido-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yamanouchi, Masuji, Baron ('07)** Mem. House of Peers, and ex-Commander Kure Admiralty was b. '60 in Musashi; entered the Naval Academy in '73; Midshipman in '79; Sub-Lieut. '84; dispatched to France and Germany '84 to study naval arsenal affairs; on commission to bring home the Matsushima built in France '90; rewarded in '92 for his invention of safety device of gun and gun-frame; proceeded to England '93-'94 and again to England in Oct. of the latter year, returning home in July '95; Naval Constructor-Commander '97, and Captain '9[illegible]; Rear-Adm. '02; his work in steel foundry affair and making of plug of Kure type was officially appreciated in '03; Chief of Construction Section, Kure '03; Vice-Adm. '03; Commander of Kure Ad'ty '06-'09; Pres. of the Japan Steel Works 1910-'14. Add. Hiyoshi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yamanouchi, Toyokage, Marquis** Capt. of Infantry (ret.), was b. in '75 in the house which formerly held the fief of Tosa, graduated from the Military Cadet School in '00 and has since been attached to the General Staff; proceeded to France for study '09 where he fell ill and returned home in '10. Married in '08 Princess Sachiko, eldest daughter of Fushimi. Add. Yoyogi, near Tokyo. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yamao, Yozo, Viscount (cr. '87)** was b. '37 in Yamaguchi, and was one of those young men who with late Prince Ito and Marquis Inouye proceeded to England on the eve of the Restoration for study, returned home in '70 and appointed Secretary at the Public Works Department and finally rose to be its Minister in '80; subsequently appointed Vice-President of the former Senate Councillor at the Court, President of the Legislative Bureau, Lord Steward to the House of Arisugawa and Kitashirakawa, etc. Resigned the official service in '93 and has since been leading retired life Add. Toriizaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n224/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yamasaki, Shoroku** Dir. Debt Bureau, Finance, was b. in '68 at Saga; graduated from Imperial Tokyo Univ. (Law) '96 and at the same time, passed the higher civil service examination, Tax Inspector in '97 attached to the Taxation Office at Nagasaki, transferred to the Head Office to be made its Secretary '00, Customs Chief in '07-'10. Add. Finance Dep't. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yamashita, Gentaro** Vice-Admiral, Deputy Chief of the Naval Staff Board and Mem. of the Adm. Council since 1914, and President of the Naval Academy '10-'14, was b. in 1863 in Yamagata-ken; graduated from the Naval Academy, 1883, 2nd Sub-Lieutenant 1885; Commander 1898; Captain 1903; Rear-Admiral 1908; Vice-Admiral 1912. Was Member of the Naval General Staff; on the Staff of the Imperial Headquarters, Chief of Staff to the 1st Squadron and then to the Sasebo Naval Station, Chief of the 2nd Section of the Naval Materials Dep't, etc. Add. Naval Staff Board, Tokyo. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yamawaki, Gen** Mem. House of Peers and ex-Pres. of Adm. Litigation Court, b. in Fukui '49, studied in Germany '70-'72. He afterward filled various high civil posts and occupied the Presidency '06-'13. Add. Hinoki-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yamawaki, Haruki** ex-Dir. of Monopoly Bureau, Formosa, was b. '71 in Kyoto, adopted son and heir of the above; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. in '99 and passed the higher civil service examination the same year; official in the Dep't of Agr. and Com., first as Per. Sec. to the Minister, then as Secretary and Sectional Chief; Commiss. Anglo-Japanese Exhibition; Chief Comis., Panama Exh. in '14. Add. Hirakawa-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yamaya, Taiin** Vice-Admiral, President of the Naval College since 1913, b. was in Keio 1866 in Tokyo; Educ: graduated from the Naval Academy 1886 and then the Naval College 1897; Navigating-Lieutenant of the transport Saikyomaru (during the Japan-China war), 1899; Captain 1905; Chief of Staff to the 4th Squadron during the Russo-Japanese war; Rear-Admiral, 1909; chief of the Bureau of Personnel '09-'13. Add. Hommura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yanagisawa, Yasutoshi** Count, Mem. House of Peers, Mem. of Japanese Census Commission, Consulting Statist to the Cabinet, Mem. of International Statistical Society, hon. Mem. of Royal Statistic Society (England) Pres. of the First Mutual Life Assurance Co. etc., was b. 1870 in Echigo, son of Lord Mitsu-aki, scion of the House of Yanagisawa which hereditarily held the fief of Koriyama, Yamato; adopted '86 as son and heir of the main house, and succeeded to the title '93; grad. at the Peers' College with honour 94; sent by the Imperial Court to Germany for study and attended the Universities of Berlin, Vienna, and Strassburg, Brussels, Paris, studying statistics; meanwhile appointed on the suite of H.H. Prince Kan-in who was sent to attend the Diamond Jubilee of Emperor Francis Joseph; returned home in 1900; attended the International Statistical Conference six times as representative of Japan, from that at Christian in 1899 and the latest at the Hague '11. Add. Tamachi, Hachichome, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yanagiya, Usaburo** Paymaster-Lieutenant (ret.), Vice-Gov. of the Japan Hypothec Bank, b. in 1867 in Nagasaki-ken. Educ. graduated from the College of Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., 1890. Entered the Bank of Japan immediately after graduation; travelled Europe in 1895; was appointed Chief of the Issue Dep't. Auditor to the New York Agency, Chief of the National Loan Bureau; resigned 1913, and appointed Dir. of the Hypothec Bank; present post '13. Add. Shio-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n225/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Yano, Fumio** Dir. of Kinjo Middle School, author, and retired diplomat was b. 1850 in Oita-prefecture; graduated from the Keiogijuku, 1870; entered the central Government as Secretary to the Treasury, 1878; resigned with Count Okuma in 1881 and took charge of the Hochi Shimbun in company with the late Mokichi Fujita; took part in the formation of the Kaishinto (Progressive party); made tour in Europe and America, '86-'88; Master of Ceremonies and Chief of the Bureau of Imperial Tombs, '90-'96; Envoy Extraordinary and Minister Plenipotentiary to China, '98-'97; placed on the retired list in 1899. He has written "Keikoku Bidan" story of ancient Greece; "Ukishiro Monogatari" (a marine novel); "News Society," etc. Add. Harajuku, Tokyo-fu. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yano, Tsuneia** Man. Dir. of the 1st Mutual Life Insur. Co., was b. '65 in Okayama; grad. the Third Higher School (Medical Branch Okayama) in '89; entered the Japan Life Insur. Co. and in '93 made manager of the Kyosai Life Insur. Co.; went over to Belgium to attend the International Congress of Actuaries held in Sept. '95; and afterward entered a German Life Insur. Co. to receive its business training; was appointed an official in the Dep't of Agr. and Com., but soon resigned to start the present Co. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yashiro, Rokuro** Vice-Adm., Min. of Navy, b. '60 in Aichi-ken; Naval Ensign in '85, and through rapid promotion was elevated to Captain in '01 and finally to the present rank; and long served as Staff-Officer of the Standing Squadron, Attache to the Legation at St. Petersbuag, and Vice Com. of the Yashima, commanding the Asama in late war; Director of Naval Staff College '12-'13; Com. of Mai-zuru Admiralty '13-'14. Add. Navy Office. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yasoshima, Chikanori** Manager of the Shibusawa's Office, Dir. of the Shinagawa Fire Brick Co., Shibusawa Ware House Co. etc., was b. '73 in Uwajima, Iyo; grad. Tokyo Higher Commercial School in '94; appointed Per. Sec. to Baron Shibusawa. He travelled once in Europe and America with the Baron. Add. Shirokane-Daimachi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yasuda, Zenjiro** a millionaire who sprang from obscurity to the present affluence, greatest perhaps next to the Mitsui and the Mitsubishi, b. '38 in Toyama-ken, and was an apprentice at a shop in Tokyo and finally opened an exchange shop on his own account. This was enlarged in scope and developed into a bank. He has founded several other banks and has moreover lent his powerful backing in the starting of the Tokyo Fire Insurance Co., the Imp. Marine Insurance Co. and Kyosai Life Insurance Co. Being a public-spirited man he does not spare to furnish fund to any useful undertaking. The fund required in the Osaka Harbour Works was almost wholly supplied by him. He is a business man of extraordinary integrity and diligence. Add. Yokoami-cho, Honjo, Tokyo. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yasuhiro, Ban-ichiro** ex-Dir. of Legislative Bureau, Crown Mem. of House of Peers, was b. '59 in Fukuoka-ken. Grad. the Keiogijuku, the Chuo Shoin at Hongkong, and finally from Cambridge Univ. in England; Prof. in the 3rd Higher Sch. in '89; Secretary to the Cabinet; Councillor to the Legislrtion Bureau; President of the Bureau for Common Schools in '97; Chief Secretary to the Cabinet '98; nominated Mem. of House of Peers in '00; Vice-Min. of the Dep't of Agr. and Com. in '01 resigned in '11. Add. Nishi-Kata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yasukawa, Kei-ichiro** highest tax-payer, mine-owner, Dir of several cos., was b. 1849 in Chikuzen; studied at the Keiogijuku early in the 80th; has made a fortune in coal-mining; founded single-handed the Meiji Semmon College in 1908 by contributing 3,000,000 yen, and has been rewarded for it with 3rd class of the Sacred Treasure. Add. Wakamatsu, Fukuoka. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yoda, Kotaro** Lieut.-Gen., Commander of the 11th Division, was b. 1853 at Kyoto; Sub-Lieut. 1877; studied in France '86-'90: Major 1892 and instructor at the Cadet School; Col. 1897 and Dir. of Toyama Military School till 1903; Major, Gen. '03 and took part in the late war as Com. of the 4th Brigade, Infantry; Lieut.-Gen. 1910. Add. Zentsuji, Sanuki. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yokoi, Jikei** Dr. Agr., Prof. Agr. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. 60 in Kumamoto; grad. from above Coll. 83; studied in Germany '99-'02. Add. Aoyama Minamicho. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yokota, Hideo** D.C.L. (Japan), Judge of Supreme Court since '01, b. in Nagano-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. class '88; sat at Tokyo, Chiba, Hakodate District Courts, and Tokyo and Hakodate Appeal Courts before his transfer to the present post; teaches at the Waseda Univ., being an authority on claims; was sent abroad on official mission '09. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yokota, Kuniomi** Pres. of the Supreme Court since 1906, was b. '50 in Oita-ken; entered the judical service '76, Public Procurator '80, the acting. Dir. Criminal Bureau and Sec. of the Dep't which post he resigned to study law in Europe '86-'91; was appointed on return Pub. Pro. of the Tokyo Appeal Court, Councillor, then Vice-Min. of Justice '96, Proc.-Gen. '98, Chief Pub. Proc. of Tokyo Appeal Court '99; Proc.-Gen. '04. Add. Kagurazaka, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yokoyama, Matajiro** Geologist, D. Sc. (Japan), Prof. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ., was b. '60 in Nagasaki; grad. Geology in the Imp. Tokyo Univ. and was subsequently appointed an official at the Dep't of Agr. and Com. in '82; sent to Germany to prosecute further study in '86; appointed Prof. at his alma mater on returning home. Add. Higashi-Goken-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yokura, Toryu** veterinarian, principal and proprietor of the Azabu Veterinary School, was b. '61 in Kagoshima; studied veterinary science at the Agr. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ., and further in America; appointed Prof. in the Agr. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '87, which he resigned some years ago to start his own veterinary school. Add. Sanko-cho, Shirokane, Tokyo. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yonei, Genjiro** Prop. of the Yonei firm, Dir. of the Meiji Gum Co., the Kirin Beer Brewing Co., etc., was b. '61 in Okayama-ken; grad. the Keiogijuku; then entered the business circle and established an import business at Ginza, the "Yonei Shoten." Add. Uchisaiwai-cho, Koj., T. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yoneyama, Umekichi** Mng. Dir. of the Mitsui Bank, was b. 1868 at Numazu; studied in America '90-'95, and entered the Nippon R'ly Co., removed to the Mitsui Bank which sent him abroad 1900 to inspect banking business 1902; chief of the Yokohama Branch 1908 and next that at Osaka 08; Mng-Dir. '10. Add. Takanawa-Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n227/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Yoshida, Sakuya** Minister to Siam since June '08, b. '59 in Higo, attaché in '86, appointed to The Hague in '83, Per. Secretary to Minister of Education in '93 and then Prof. in Third High School, 2nd Legation Sec. at Vienna in '98, removed to The Hague in '00, and transferred to Vienna in '01, of which post he was relieved in '04. Add. Bangkok. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Togo** Dr. Lit. (Japan), historian and histographer, Prof. at Waseda University, was b. 1864 in Niigata; has risen to eminence by self-study; wrote on historical subjects to the Yomiuri and completed the Dictionary of Japanese Geography in 1909 after 16 yars work, the Reverse History of Japan (pyramidal with basis for modern time and summit for ancient time). Add. Yarai, U., T, — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Yosaku** Per. Secretary to Foreign Minister since '96 and attaché of the Board of Ceremonies, Court, b. '50; clerk at Foreign Office in '77-'86, attaché in '87, élève Consul at Manila in '92 and then attaché in Berlin. Add. Foreign Office, Tokyo. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))

**Yoshihara, Suburo** Pres. of the Oriental Development Co. 1913; ex-Vice-Min. of Home Affairs, was b. '54 in Chiba-ken; appointed a Secretary of the House of Representatives, '90; then Secretary in prefectural Government of Saitama, Osaka and Aichi, Governor of Kagawa-ken and of Okayama-ken. Director of Local Administration Bureau, Home Office '03; Vice-Minister in Jan. '06, Vice-Pres., Oriental Development Co., Seoul '08. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))

**Yoshii, Yukei** Dir. of the Bank of Japan, was b. '63 in Kagoshima. Early entered the Bank of Japan in which he has gradually distinguished himself to be given an important position of its Director. Add. Kita-igacho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))

**Yoshikawa, Akimasa** Count, (cr. Vis. '96 and Count '07), Vice-President of Privy Council since '12, b. '41 at Tokushima; entered the civil service early in the Meiji era and after having held various posts in the Treasury, home office and elsewhere was appointed the Gov. of Tokyo-fu and then Vice-Min. of Home Affairs. Then began his ministerial career, first as Minister of Edu. '90; Justice, '93-'96; Home Office, '91-'96; Communications twice '98-'00 and '01-'3; Home Office, '04, which last he had to resign in connection with the Sept. 5th anti-Peace Agitation in Tokyo. Add. Miyamura-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))

**Yoshimatsu, Shigetaro** Vice-Adm., Com. of the Kure Adm. since 1914 was b. in Kochi-ken '59; appointed Sub-Lieut. in '83; was at one time Staff Officer of the Kure Adm., Chief of the First Bureau, Chief Staff Office of Sasebo Adm., Com. of the Naniwa and Takasago, and some others. Rear-Adm. in '07, Vice-Adm. 09; Pres. of Naval-Staff Col. in '01; Com. 2nd Squad. in '11-'12, Chief of Edu. Board '12-'14. Add. Kure. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))

**Yui, Mitsuye** Lieut-Gen., Dep't Chief of Staff Board, was b. in Kochi-ken; Sub-Lt., '82; graduated from Military Staff College, studied in England, Vice-Chief of Gen. Staff of 2nd Army in Russo-Japan War; then Com. of 1st Regm't of Imp. Guard; next Comm. of 27 Brigade; present post. Add. War Office. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 228](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n228/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Zumoto, Motosada** Prop. and Dir. of the Japan Times, started by him in '97, and only English paper owned and conducted by the Japanese, was b. '62 in Hoki Province, and grad. Sapporo Agr. Col. '84; was long on the staff of the Japan Mail; entered civil service for a short while and was twice appointed Per. Sec. to the late Prince Ito when he was Premier; frequently visited Europe and America; was during the late war a resident correspondent of the London Standard in Tokyo, accompanied the late Prince Ito to Seoul as non-official mem. of the Residency-Gen., and in January 1906 he took over the Seoul Press formerly owned by an Englishman. Withdrew from it in '09, to start the Oriental Information Agency in New York city. Returned to Tokyo in December '10 to assume the control of the Japan Times. Add. The Japan Times, Tokyo. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 229](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n229/mode/1up))

**Zushi, Tamiyoshi** Director of the Taiwan Sugar Co., was b. '54 at Kagoshima; studied at an American Col. '73-'79; Sec. of the former Public Works Dep't '79; transferred to the R'ly Bureau '83 and remained in the service as Dep't Dir. till '09 when he was placed on retired list. Add. Ochiai-mura, near Tokyo. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 229](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n229/mode/1up))

### Bankers

**Chosen Ginko** (Bank of Chosen), K. K., Seoul, Korea. See Chap. on Chosen. — p.  ([scan leaf 766](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n766/mode/1up))

**Dai Hyaku Ginko** (100th Bank), K. K., Yorozucho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. '77, Directors, K. Ikeda (Pres.) S. Asada (Mng.) — p.  ([scan leaf 766](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n766/mode/1up))

**Dai Ichi Ginko** (1st Bank), K. K., Kabutocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. The first private Bank in Japan est. July '73. Directors, Baron E. Shibusawa (Pres.), Y. Sasaki (Gen.-Mng.) — p.  ([scan leaf 766](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n766/mode/1up))

**Kitahama Ginko** K. K., Kitahama, Osaka. Est. March '97. Directors, S. Iwashita (Pres.), S. Kotsuka (Mng. Dir.) — p.  ([scan leaf 766](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n766/mode/1up))

### Exporters & Importers

**Akita Timber Co., K. K., Noshiro, Akita** Est. Apr. '07, amalgamating the Noshiro Hanzai, Akita Seizai and Noshiro Zaimoku Cos.; capital ¥ 1,200 p. u.; Directors; N. Isaka (Pres.), D. Takemura, S. Kikuchi (Mng. Dirs.) — p. 721 ([scan leaf 768](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n768/mode/1up))

**Hunter E. H. & Co** Hirama-cho, Kobe. Formed 1868 by E. H. Hunter; first engaged in rice export business but since many years begun to import machineries and also engaged antimony refining business; have special connection with Osaka Iron Works. Props. E. H. Hunter, Ryutaro Hunter. — p. 721 ([scan leaf 768](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n768/mode/1up))

**Kawamata Kempu Seiren K, K** Masagocho, Yokohama. Leading silk exporters, est. '99 with capital ¥ 100 p. u. Brand: Kawamata Habutaye, Deer brand. Directors; K. Komatsu, (Mng. Dir.) — p. 721 ([scan leaf 768](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n768/mode/1up))

**Maruzen K. K** Publishers, book-sellers and stationers. Tori-Sanchome, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1880, capital 350 p. u.; connections, world wide. Directors; K. Oyaidzu. Branches; Osaka, Kyoto and Fukuoka. — p. 721 ([scan leaf 768](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n768/mode/1up))

**Meidiya, K. K** Importers and dealers in groceries, provisions and sole agent of Kirin Beer, Nunobiki Tansan Mineral Water, Jas. Buchanan's Whisky, J. Brown's Scotch Whisky, etc. Head Office: Honcho, Yokohama. Est. 1885 by late Mr. H. Isono, recognized as partnership unlimited company Aug. 1903; changed into limited liability company May '11. Warranted purveyors to the Imperial Household and the Imperial Navy. Capital ¥ 500, Branches; Tokyo, Osaka, Kobe, Moji and Seoul. Directors; G. Yonei (Pres.), C. Isono (Vice-Pres.), M. Miyaji (Mng. Dir.), K. Sano (Dir.). — p. 721 ([scan leaf 768](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n768/mode/1up))

**Sankyo & Co., K. K** druggists and chemists, Muromachi, Nihombashi, Tokyo. S. A.; removed to Tokyo in '01; incorporated Shiohara's firm with capital ¥ 500 in '07; K. K. '13 with capital 1212 p. u.; Dr. J. Takamine (Pres.) — p. 722 ([scan leaf 769](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n769/mode/1up))

**Yokohama Nursery Co., K. K** Nakamura-cho Yokohama. Est. '00; exporter of nursery stocks, seed and plants; capital ¥ 500. Branches: London and New York, etc. Directors, U. Suzuki (Mng. Dir.), S. Iida (Mng.) — p. 722 ([scan leaf 769](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n769/mode/1up))

**Yonei & Co** General Importers and exporters, Ginza, Tokyo. Est. in 1887 by late K. Isono under the name of Isono & Co.; contractors to the several Government Depts.; agent for many foreign cos. Branches: Osaka, Kobe, Seoul, &c. Prop. G. Yonei. — p. 722 ([scan leaf 769](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n769/mode/1up))

### Mining

**Furukawa Mining Co., Gm. K** Yayesucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Late Ichibei Furukawa's copper works reorganized as such in '05 with capital ¥ 5,000; own Ashio and six other copper mines besides Innai silver mine; manufacture copper wire, bar and cokes; Directors, T. Furukawa (Pres.), C. Kimura, K. Inouye (Dirs.) — p. 722 ([scan leaf 769](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n769/mode/1up))

**Hoden Petroleum Co., K. K** Nagaoka, Niigata-ken. Est. 1898; one of the leading petroleum cos; capital ¥ 13,325 p. u.; Directors, M. Yamada (Pres.), T. Watanabe, T. Ikeda (Mng. Dirs.) — p. 722 ([scan leaf 769](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n769/mode/1up))

**Hokkaido Tanko Kisen K. K** (Colliery and S. S. Co.), Kamiyamagiwara, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. Nov. 1900 and was first engaged in the business of coal mining and railway but with the railway nationalization in Oct. '06, expanded the colliery work, shipping service, also undertake the manufacture of cokes and forestry business. In conjunction with Messrs. Armstrong and Vickers, the Co. opened a steel foundry styled the Nippon Seikosho. Capital ¥ 18,000 p. u.; Directors, T. Dan (Chairman, Board Dirs.), T. Uno, T. Isomura (Mng. Dirs.) — p. 722 ([scan leaf 769](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n769/mode/1up))

**Iriyama Saitan K. K** (Colliery Co.), Yagembori, Tokyo. Est. May '95; capital ¥ 870 p. u.; has coal mine in Fukushima prefecture. Directors, S. Go (Pres.), N. Uchida (Mng. Dir.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

Kuhara (Pres.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Mitsui Mining Co., K. K** Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo; Est. 1911; capital 5,000 p. u.; Directors, M. Mitsui (Pres.), G. Mitsui (Mng. Dir.) See the Mitsui under heading, "General." — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Nippon Petroleum Co., K. K** Osu, Niigata-ken. Est. 1888; own Kurokawa wells (see Chap. Mining); capital 12,500 p. u.; Directors, H. Naito (Pres.), O. Matsukata (Mng. Dir.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

### Stock Exchange And Brokers

**Fukushima Shokai, Gs. K** Stock-brokers, Aomonocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. Dec. 1905 succeeding Mr. N. Fukushima's business; sent a representative abroad in 1907 and has wide connection in Europe and America. Departments; Bonds, Financial and Trust. Capital, yen 1,000. Directors, N. Fukushima (Pres.), N. Maeda (Mng.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Koike Gs. K** Stock-brokers, Kabutocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. April '97 by K. Koike; reorganized in '07 as ltd. partnership with capital yen 1,000; have regular connection with England and other European business centres. K. Koike (Pres.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Beikoku Shohin Torihikijo** (Rice and Produce Exchange of Tokyo). K. K., first formed in '76; capital yen 100; increased to yen 400 in '93, to yen 1,500 in '08 when the Tokyo Produce Exchange was amalgamated; now yen 3,250; Directors, K. Nedzu (Pres.), Y. Iriye (Mng.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Kabushiki Torihikijo** (Stock Exchange), K. K., Kabutocho, Tokyo. Est. in 1876; capital yen 8,000 p. u.; Directors; S. Go (Pres.), S. Tsumoda. (Dir.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

### Steel And Iron Works

**Ikegai Ironworks** Honshiba, Tokyo. Est. June '06 with capital yen 250 by the Ikegai family. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Niigata Ironworks, K. K** Niigata. Est. 1910; capital yen 1,000 p. u. Directors, T. Yamaguchi (Pres.), M. Sasamura (Mng. Dir.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Nippon Ironworks, K. K** Muroran, Hokkaido. Est. Nov. 1908; capital 15,000 p. u.; Directors, A. Takasaki (Pres.), S. Kondo (Mng.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Shibaura Engineering works, K. K** Shiba, Tokyo. Est. July 1875; gradually enlarged since 1892, when the works came into possession of the Mitsui family; reorganized in 1904 as K. K., with capital yen 1,000, the members of the Mitsui as principal shareholders; at present capital 2,750 p. u.; Directors, M. Mitsui (Pres.), R. Kishi, S. Kobayashi (Mng. Dirs.) — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

**Osaka Ironworks** see Chap. on Mercantile Marine. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))

### Shipping And Dockyards

**Hakodate Dockyard, K. K** Hakodate. Est. '06 with capital of yen 570 p. u. Mng. Dir. T. Kawada. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 770](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n770/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kawasaki Dockyards, K. K** Kobe. The forerunner of the works was called the Tokyo Tsukiji Dockyard, founded in May 1878 by Mr. Shozo Kawasaki. Reorganized in Oct. 1896 as K. K. with capital of yen 2,000; increased to yen 8,600; Directors, Kojiro Matankata (Pres.), Yoshitaro Kawasaki (Vice-Pres.) See Chaps. on Mercantile Marine, and the Army and the Navy. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Nan-yo Yusen K. K** Kobe. Former Nan-yo Gumi, reorganized as such in 1915. See chap. on Mercantile Marine. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Nippon Yusen K. K** Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. See Chap. on Mercantile Marine. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Nisshin Kisen K. K** (Japan-China S. S. Co.), Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. See Chap. on Mercantile Marine. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Osaka Shosen K. K** Tomishimacho, N., Osaka. See Chap. on Mercantile Marine. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Ishikawajima Dockyards, K. K** Ishikawajima, Tokyo. Shipbuilding and Engineering works; one of the oldest private establishments; reorganized in Jan. 1887 as K. K. with expanded business; capital yen 1,020 p. u. Directors; K. Watanabe (Pres.), T. Uchida (Mng.) — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Kisen K. K** Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. See Chap. on Mercantile Marine. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

### Railways And Cars

**Dai Nippon Kido K. K** (light-railway Co.), Tsukiji, Tokyo. Est. '08 amalgamating eight light railway Co. in different prefectures; capital yen 167 p. u.; Directors, W. Amenomiya, R. Otaki. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Kawagoye Railway Co., K. K** Kawagoye. Est June '93 with capital yen 402 p. u. open milage 18m. 36c.; average receipts per mile about yen 17,000 against average expenses yen 10,000. Directors, S. Iwata (Pres.), Y. Nagata (Mng.) — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Kisha Seizo Gs. K** Shimaya-cho, Osaka. Est. Sept. '96 by the late Viscomt K. Inouye; contract rolling stock and also undertake general iron wares. Director, K. Hiraoka. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Narita Tetsudo K. K** Narita, Chiba. Est. Nov. 1895; capital yen 2,425 p. u. open to traffic between Narita and Sakura (45 m.). Directors; T. Asada, (Pres.), W. Saito (Mng. Dir.) — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Nippon Sharyo Seizo K. K** Car builder and ironworks, Atsuta, Nagoya. Est. Oct. 1896; capital yen 750 p. u. Lately united with the Osaka Loco. Works and others to monopolize the orders of Gov't Rlys in rolling stock. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Tobu Railway Co., K. K** Koume-Kawaracho, Honjo, Tokyo. One of the hopeful railway cos.; est. Nov. 1897; capital yen 3,161 p. u.; Directors, K. Nezu (Res.), D. Yoshino (Mng. Dir.) — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

### Printing And Publishing Houses

**Hakubun-Kwan** publishing house, Honcho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. founded 1887 by the late Sahei Ohashi, father of the present proprietor, Shintaro Ohashi. Has contributed not a little to the progress of learning and intellectual activity by endeavouring the cost of publication and supplying the publications at lowest possible price. He issues 60 kinds of magazine and 6,000 varieties of books, over 80 varieties of serial works, 20 of them still in course of publication. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))

**Kyobun-Kwan** (Methodist Publishing House), Ginza, Tokyo. leading book-sellers and stationers in Tokyo, also undertakes printing. Agent, Ginn & Co., Boston. E. J. Ingrehart (Gen. Mng.) — p. 724 ([scan leaf 771](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n771/mode/1up))


## Appendices

**Shimbi Shoin, K. K** publishers of fine art work, Sakana-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Reorganized in 1906 as K. K., succeeding Mr. Tajima's private establishment. Awarded gold medals at home and foreign exhibitions. Capital yen 150 p. u. S. Tajima (Mng. Dir.) See Chap. on Arts and Crafts. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Printing Co., K. K** Printer, Kabutocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. One of the leading printing shops in Tokyo. Reorganized as K. K., Mar. 1896 with capital of yen 150; contractors to several Government Departments. Awarded gold medals at home exhibitions. Mg. Dir. C. Hoshino. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Tsukiji Kappan Seizojo, K. K** Tsukiji, Tokyo. Pioneer printing and type founder, est. 1873; capital yen 300; awarded gold medals at foreign and home exhibitions for art printing and type-founding; contractors to Government. President S. Nomura. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

### Spinning Mills

**Fuji Gasu Boseki K. K** gassed yarn, Oshima-machi, Tokyo. Est. '97 with capital yen 8,000 afterwards purchased Onagigawa cotton mill and others; capital yen 12,000 p. u., reserve funds yen 2,684; have factories at Oshiage, Oyama Nos. 1, 3, 4 etc. for cotton spinning, Hodogaya Oyama No. 2 for silk spinning and Onagigawa for cotton spinning and weaving. Directors, Dr. T. Wada (Pres.), E. Kawasaki (Dir.) — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Jomo Muslin K. K** Tatebayashi, Gumma. Est. Mar. '02 with capital yen 1,000; at present 2,498 p. u.; Directors, S. Kohayashi (Pres.) — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Kanegafuchi Cotton Spinning Co., K. K** Sumidamura, Tokyo. Leading cotton mill in Japan: Est. May '87 with capital yen 1,000, afterward increased to yen 15,927 by purchasing or amalgamating several mills, at present 14,966 p. u. Have branch mills at Sumida-mura, Hyogo and ten others. Directors, H. Hibiya (Pres.), Y. Muto (Mng. Dir.), H. Yamaguchi (Mng.) — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))
