**Osaka Godo Cotton Spinning Co., K. K** Dojimahamacho, Osaka. Est. 1900, capital ¥ 4,000 p.u. Directors, F. Taniguchi (Pres.), H. Akiyama (Dir.) — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Settsu Cotton Spinning Co., K. K** Kizugawa, Osaka. Est. 1689; capital ¥ 3076 p.u.; Directors. J. Takeo (Pres.), M. Ito (Dir.) — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Cotton Spinning Co., K. K** Higashi-Daikucho, Fukagawa, Tokyo. Est. 1887; capital ¥ 4,800 p.u.; Directors, T. Kakinuma (Pres.), S. Miyashima (Mng. Dir.) — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Toyo Cotton Spinning Co., K. K** Yokkaichi, Mie. Formed June 1914 by amalgamation of Mie Cot. Sp. Co. (est. 86, cap. ¥ 7,768 p.u.) and Osaka Cot. Sp. Co. (est. '82, cap. ¥ 5000 p.u.): Directors, J. Yamabe (Pres.), T. Oka (Gen. Mng.), O. Shoji (Vice Gen. Mng.) — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

### Curios, Jewels, Toilet Articles, Etc.

**Ando Shippo-ten** enamel ware, Ginza, Tokyo. Est. '82 by J. Ando. Average annual output is reported about ¥ 30,000. Branch; Harbart, U.S.A. Awarded with Gold and Silver medals at Paris, St. Louis, Anglo-Japanese Fairs, etc. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))

**Ikeda Gm. K** Curio merchant, Shimmonzen, Kyoto. Formed Mar, '96 succeeding former Ikeda firm; capital ¥ 300 with eight partners; branch at Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 772](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n772/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Isekichi Shoten** , toilet articles, Yokoami, Higashi-Ryogoku, Tokyo. One of the oldest establishments dating from Tokugawa regency; speciality Tooth powders styled "Takarako" "Baikosan" and "Nippon-san." Export large amount to China, Korea, etc. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Kitashimizu Katsuzo** , Tomino-koji, Kyoto. Toys, especially dolls, largely exported; awarded high prizes at Paris and several domestic fairs. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Mikimoto Shinjuten** , jewels and pearls, Ginza, Tokyo. Has piscina at Ago and Gokasho bays near Toba, and cultures pearl by patented process invented by the proprietor of the firm. Awarded grand prize medals at foreign and domestic exhibitions; has gold and silver smith depts. Prop., K. Mikimoto. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Miyamoto Shoko** , silver smith and dealer, Yazaemoncho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1882; contractor to several Government Depts.; awarded gold and silver medals at foreign and domestic exhibitions. Prop., K. Miyamoto. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

### Dry Goods Stores, Etc.

**Mitsukoshi Gofukuten** , K. K., Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. A leading and the oldest dry goods stores with a history of three hundred years and also pioneer department store. Reorganized as K. K. with capital of ¥ 2,000 in which the Mitsui family holds larger share. Conducting mail order business for about 20 years, known as "Harrods in Tokyo," or "Marshal Field in Tokyo." Mng. Dir. O. Hibi. Branches; Osaka, Kyoto, Seoul and Dairen. Awarded gold medals at foreign and domestic exhibitions. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Shirokiya** , dry goods store, Nihombashi, Tokyo. One of the oldest dry goods stores in Tokyo, founded about the middle of the Tokugawa era. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Takashimaya** , Gs. K., Kyoto and Tokyo. Formerly private firm run by Mr. Shinshichi Iida, one of the largest dry goods merchants in Kyoto and also manufacturer of high grade fabrics; reorganized in 1909 as K. K., Prop. S. Iida. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

### Brewery

**Dai Nippon Beer Brewery Co., K. K** , Meguro, near Tokyo. Formed Jan. 1906 by amalgamating Ebisu, Sapporo and Asahi breweries; capital ¥ 7,520 p. u.; Directors, K. Makoshi (Pres.), T. Uyemura (Mng. Dir.) Brands, "Ebisu" "Sapporo," "Asahi" "Munchener," etc. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Kabuto Beer Brewery Co., K. K** , Ginza, Tokyo. Est. Sept. '96, with capital ¥ 2,448 p. u.; Directors, K. Nezu (Pres.), T. Haruna (Mng.) Brand "Kabuto." — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Kirin Beer Brewery Co., K. K** , Bund, Yokohama. Founded by some foreigners and reorganized in '07 into K. K. with Japanese and foreign share-holders; capital ¥ 2,500 p. u.; Directors, G. Yonei (Mng. Dir.), R. Kondo (Dir.). Brands, "Lager," "Pilsna," etc. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Nishinomiya Sake Brewery Co., K. K** , Nishinomiya, Hyogo. Est. 1889; capital ¥ 400 p. u.; Directors, J. Iida (Dir.), T. Anami (Mng.) — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Mineral Water Co., K. K** , Higashi-Umeda, Osaka. Est. Feb. 1907, capital ¥ 600. Own Mitsuya & Peacock brands of Hirano table water containing natural carbonic acid gas, obtained from a spring at Hirano-village, near Ikeda station of the Hankaku railway. Awarded gold medal at St. Louis Exposition in 1904. Directors; T. Hiraga (Pres.), S. Nakatani (Mng. Dir.) — p. 726 ([scan leaf 773](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n773/mode/1up))

Morinaga (Pres.), H. Matsuzaki (Gen. Mng.), T. Kakujima (Mng. of Osaka Branch.) — p. 727 ([scan leaf 774](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n774/mode/1up))

### General

**Fujitagumi, Gm. K** Awajicho, Osaka. Undertakes reclamation, forestry and mining works; own Kosaka, Omori and Zuiho mines; reclamation office, Kojima-wan, Okayama; Forestry office, Ekitaizan, Formosa, capital ¥ 6,000; Directors, Heitaro Fujita. — p. 727 ([scan leaf 774](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n774/mode/1up))

**Mitsubishi Gs. K** Mining, shipbuilding, iron works, banking, &c. The Company was known as the Mitsubishi Mail Steamship Co. until 1885 then amalgamated with the Kyodo Unyu Kaisha, forming present Nippon Yusen Kaisha, to which was handed over shipping business. In 1893, was reorganized as partnership unlimited company, capital ¥ 15,000. Directors; Baron H. Iwasaki, Pres., Baron K. Iwasaki, Vice-Pres., R. Toyokawa and K. Nambu, Gen. Managers; Department Chiefs; S. Sho, Internal; R. Mizutani, Shipbuilding; K. Kimura, C. Harada, Mining; T. Eguchi, Business; K. Kimura, General; Z. Kirishima, Real Estate. — p. 727 ([scan leaf 774](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n774/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Mitsui Gm. K** Controlling centre of all the undertakings by the Mitsui, including the Mitsui Mining department, which was established in 1892; own the Miike, Tagawa, Yamano and Hondo Collieries, Kamioka Silver mine and Iwanobori Sulphur mine. Awarded gold medals at foreign and local exhibitions. 1/16 of the total output of coal in Japan is the production of the company. — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))

**Mitsui Bussan K. K** General importers and exporters; Surugacho, Nihonbashi, Tokyo. Established in the latter part of 10th century as a dry goods store, and the Mitsui Bussan was organized in 1876 to undertake general trading but more particularity foreign trade. Reorganized in Oct. '09 as Joint Stock Company, with other enterprises, all practically owned by the 11 branches of the Mitsui family; capital (fully p.u.) ¥ 20,000. Directors; T. Mitsui, Pres., S. Watanabe, K. Fukui, and M. Enjise, Mng. Dirs. Branches; London, New York, &c. connection, world wide. — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))

**Okuragumi, K. K** General exporters and importers, Ginza, Tokyo. The firm organized as a partnership with Mr. Kihachiro Okura, well-known merchant in the country, as president, in 1873. Reorganized in a joint stock Co. in Dec. '11 with capital yen 10,000. The firm is the first Japanese firm engaged in foreign business; is divided into manufacturing, spinning, mining, enginering, electrical, railway, shipping, forestry, architecture, and insurance departments. Directors; Kihachiro Okura, Pres; K. Takashima, C. Kadono, Kumema Okura, Kishichi Okura, and S. Takaya, Dirs. — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))

**Sumitomo Ginko** Bankers, Osaka; first established in about 1871, and reorganized in 1895 with capital of yen 1,000. Again reorganized, as a joint stock Co. in '12. Began foreign draft business in 1903. Connection with Europe and America. In the return of 1st half, 1912, the reserve fund shows yen 4,800. Branches Tokyo, Yokohama and 15 others. Proprietors, Baron K. Sumitomo, (see who's who) Mng. Dir., K. Nakada. Sec. Chiefs, S. Uyeno, S. Yoshika, N. Yashiro. The Sumitomo firm separated from the Bank and owns copper and coal mines and undertakes steel works and warehouse business in the same city. — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))

**Takata Shokai, Gm. K** Yurakucho, Marunouchi, Tokyo. General importers and exporters. Est. in 1880, succeeding Mr. Bair's business, a German merchant; reorganized in Dec. 1908, cap. yen 1,000. Contractors to Government and its connections are world wide. Branches London, New York, Osaka and Shanghai. Directors; Shinzo Takata, Nobujiro Takata and Kamakichi Takata. — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))

### Gas And Electricity

**Hakodate Hydro-Electric Co., K. K., C. L** Hakodate, Hokkaido. Est. 1905, capital yen 1328 p. u.; transportation business not yet open; Directors, J. Sonoda (Pres.), M. Hirano (Mng. Dir.) — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))

**Hiroshima Gas Works, K. K., G** Hiroshima. Est. 1908, capital yen 1,170; Directors, G. Matsukata (Pres.), T. Matsuura (Mng. Dir.) — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))

**Katsuragawa Electric Co., K. K., C. M** Ginza III, Tokyo. Est. 1910, capital yen 5599 p. u.; supplies current for the motors of the Tokyo municipal electric undertakings, as trams, lights, etc. Directors, W. Amemiya (Pres.), S. Iwata (Mng. Dir.) — p. 728 ([scan leaf 775](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1914/page/n775/mode/1up))
